NHL · Nov 9, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

McDavid ties Oates at 18 — now five games from Gretzky's assist record

By Matt

Connor McDavid picked up three assists in Edmonton's 4-2 win over Vancouver on Saturday, running his season-opening assist streak to 18 games — tying Adam Oates for the second-longest in NHL history. Only Wayne Gretzky's 23 stands ahead of him, and McDavid gets his next crack at it tonight against the Rangers.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Connor McDavid did not need to score a goal to own the night. The Edmonton Oilers captain collected three assists on Saturday as Edmonton beat the Vancouver Canucks 4-2 at Rogers Place, extending his assist streak to 18 games and tying Adam Oates for the second-longest such run in NHL history.

Leon Draisaitl did the finishing. He scored twice and added an assist, Mattias Ekholm and Jason Dickinson also scored, and Devon Levi made 20 saves in his return from injury as the Oilers (12-3-3) snapped a two-game slide. Edmonton is now 10-0-2 when scoring first this season. The Canucks (3-15-1) have lost five straight and 15 of their last 16.

The streak, in context

Only one player in NHL history has ever had an assist in more consecutive games than McDavid has right now: Wayne Gretzky, who assisted in 23 straight for the Los Angeles Kings in 1990-91. Oates did it in 18 straight for the Boston Bruins in 1992-93 — in the middle of a 97-assist season. McDavid has now caught him.

This is also the longest assist streak to open a season in league history. McDavid set that mark when he reached 13 straight games on October 28, and he has kept adding to it every night since. It is the longest season-opening streak by any active player, passing his own 17-game run from 2021-22 that he shared with Toronto's William Nylander (2023-24). He is also the sixth player ever with a point in each of his team's first 18 games of a season, and he leads the league with 47 points — 10 goals and 37 assists.

The math from here is simple and delicious. If the streak holds, McDavid ties Gretzky on November 17 against the Panthers in Florida, and breaks the record two nights later against the Lightning in Tampa. Tonight is game 19, at home against the Rangers. McDavid had a goal and an assist in Edmonton's 4-1 win at New York on November 1 — the night he became the fastest player in NHL history to reach 30 assists in a season.

How Saturday played out

Ekholm put Edmonton ahead 1-0 at 9:14 of the first, taking a one-time pass from McDavid at the front of the right circle. Max Sasson tied it with nine seconds left in the period. Dickinson regained the lead 49 seconds into the third on a backhand off his own rebound, and four minutes later McDavid sent a cross-ice feed to Draisaitl for a one-timer and a 3-1 lead — the kind of setup the NHL's own account called perfection. Brock Boeser made it 3-2 at 17:25, and Draisaitl added an empty-netter with 45 seconds left for his 16th goal of the season.

It was not a clean night for Edmonton otherwise. Zach Hyman returned after missing five games with a neck injury and had an assist. Ryan Nugent-Hopkins left 45 seconds into the second period after taking a puck to the head off a teammate's shot — coach Mike Babcock said the initial fear was a broken jaw, but Nugent-Hopkins seemed to be doing OK afterward and Babcock does not expect him to miss much time. Evan Bouchard extended his points streak to six games, and he and Draisaitl both have points in 16 of 18 games.

Babcock liked that it was tight. "This is what I expected tonight," he said. "I'd like to beat them 6-0, but the reality is this was way better for us, and it was way better that they pushed back and we had to stick to doing it right."

Why this one matters

Gretzky's records are supposed to be the untouchable ones. McDavid has spent his whole career running into them — and lately, running through them. He already owns the season-opening assist streak outright. Now he is five games from the all-time mark, with the chase playing out in real time: tonight at home, then a road trip that ends in Florida, where the record would fall.

And the Oilers need every bit of it. They are the league's highest-scoring team, but they have been winning with their captain setting the table every single night — 37 assists in 18 games, a point in all 18. The only question left is whether anyone can stop the streak before Tampa. So far, 18 teams have tried. None have.

The box score

Final score: Oilers 4, Canucks 2

McDavid: 3 assists, 18-game assist streak, 47 points (10G, 37A)

Draisaitl: 2 goals, 1 assist (16th goal of the season)

Levi: 20 saves, 7-1-0

Oilers: 12-3-3, 10-0-2 when scoring first

Canucks: 3-15-1, five straight losses

NFL · Nov 8, 2026 · 5:11 PM ET

Broncos set NFL record in 16th straight one-score game

By Matt

Denver beat Carolina 26-21 in Charlotte on Sunday for its 16th straight one-score win — a new NFL record, passing Peyton Manning's 2008-09 Colts — behind a fourth-quarter rally and Nik Bonitto's game-sealing strip-sack.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Denver Broncos just keep finding ways to win the close ones. Denver beat the Carolina Panthers 26-21 on Sunday at Bank of America Stadium in Charlotte, and the win was the Broncos' 16th straight in a one-score game — a new NFL record.

Denver is now 7-2, on a five-game winning streak, and alone in first place in the AFC West. Carolina fell to 3-5 with its third straight loss.

The record

One-score games are decided by eight points or fewer, and nobody in NFL history has survived more of them in a row than these Broncos. The previous mark was 15 straight, held by Peyton Manning's 2008-09 Indianapolis Colts — a team that went to a Super Bowl on the back of exactly that kind of late-game nerve.

Bo Nix has started all 16 games of the streak. Denver's last loss in a one-score game came in Week 3 of the 2025 season, a 23-20 defeat at the Chargers. Since then, the Broncos have won every close game they've played.

NBC Sports research noted before the game that Denver's run — counting last season's playoff overtime win over Buffalo — already tied the 2023-24 Kansas City Chiefs for the longest one-score win streak since 1950. Either way you count it, Sunday made the regular-season record Denver's alone.

What makes the streak stranger is how Denver keeps doing it. The Broncos have a minus-658 yardage differential on the season, third-worst in the NFL, per NBC Sports. They get outgained, they fall behind, and they still walk out with the win.

Overall: 7-2

Win streak: 5 games

Straight one-score wins: 16 (NFL record)

Previous record: 15, 2008-09 Colts

Last one-score loss: Week 3, 2025 — 23-20 at Chargers

How the 16th one went down

Denver dominated the first quarter and then spent the rest of the afternoon digging itself out.

First quarter — Broncos 10, Panthers 0. Denver held the ball for almost 11 minutes. The opening drive stalled at the three after a delay-of-game penalty, and Wil Lutz opened the scoring with a 26-yard field goal. On the next drive, a 22-yard catch by Jaylen Waddle and a 20-yard screen to Evan Engram set up Bo Nix's three-yard touchdown pass to Courtland Sutton.

Second quarter — Broncos 13, Panthers 7. Nix's streak of 180 passes without an interception ended when Carolina cornerback Corey Thornton picked him off in the red zone — his first pick since Week 3. The Panthers turned it into points, converting a third-and-17 on a long march that ended with Bryce Young's touchdown pass to tight end Tommy Tremble. With 1:27 left and no timeouts, Denver still got Lutz a 48-yard field goal before halftime.

Third quarter — Panthers 14, Broncos 13. Carolina opened the half with a 65-yard drive capped by Young's 15-yard touchdown to tight end Darren Waller, and the Panthers took the lead. Then they marched from their own 12-yard line and looked ready to go up by eight — until Young's pass was tipped up and safety Devon Key snatched it in the end zone for a critical turnover.

Fourth quarter — Broncos 26, Panthers 21. Down one, Denver put together a 14-play, 80-yard drive. A third-and-six completion from Nix to Sutton got the Broncos to the 20, and running back Jonah Coleman capped it with a nine-yard touchdown run. The two-point try failed, leaving it 19-14. Jonathon Cooper's first sack of the season forced a three-and-out, and Nix found wide receiver Pat Bryant wide open for a 26-yard touchdown to make it 26-14.

Carolina wouldn't go away. The Panthers converted a fourth-and-18 to keep their final touchdown drive alive, and Young's four-yard pass to Tetairoa McMillan cut it to 26-21 with 3:05 left. Then Coleman bobbled a toss from Nix, lost 18 yards, and Denver had to punt from deep in its own territory just after the two-minute warning.

The Panthers took over just shy of midfield with a real shot at the comeback. On the second play, Nik Bonitto batted the ball out of Young's hand — a strip-sack, recovered by Bonitto — and that was the game. Denver picked up a first down, knelt out the clock, and the record was theirs.

The cost

The win wasn't free. Receiver Jaylen Waddle left late in the first quarter and cornerback Riley Moss left midway through the second, both to be evaluated for possible concussions, and both were ruled out at halftime. Key briefly exited in the fourth quarter to be evaluated but returned to the game. Denver's defense was already down three of its four starting defensive backs at times in this one, per Mile High Report, and it showed — Carolina moved the ball all afternoon.

What it means

The Broncos head into the bye in Week 10 at 7-2 and in first place in the AFC West, with the NFL trade deadline on Tuesday before they host the Las Vegas Raiders in Week 11. The record is the headline, but the pattern underneath it is the real story: this team does not lose close games anymore. In 2024, Denver was 1-6 in one-score games. Now it's 16 straight wins in them, and the record book belongs to Bo Nix and a defense that keeps producing one more play than the other team.

NFL · Nov 8, 2026 · 2:10 PM ET

Ja'Marr Chase puts on a show in Madrid as Bengals beat Falcons 34-17

By Matt

Ja'Marr Chase caught 10 passes for 155 yards — 142 of them in the first half — as the Bengals beat the Falcons 34-17 in Madrid, their fourth straight win and first ever outside the United States.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Madrid got the full Ja'Marr Chase experience on Sunday. The Bengals wide receiver caught 10 passes for 155 yards and a touchdown — 142 of those yards before halftime — as Cincinnati beat the Atlanta Falcons 34-17 at Real Madrid's Santiago Bernabéu Stadium in Week 9.

The win was the Bengals' fourth straight, pushed them to 6-2, and pulled them into a tie with the Pittsburgh Steelers atop the AFC North. It was also the first win outside the United States in franchise history.

Chase took over before halftime

Atlanta scored first. Michael Penix Jr. found Charlie Woerner for a one-yard touchdown on the Falcons' opening drive, the first touchdown ever scored in an NFL game in Madrid, and the Falcons led 7-0.

Cincinnati answered in five plays. Joe Burrow hit Chase on a 47-yard touchdown strike to tie it, and the Bengals never trailed again.

Chase's first half was the story of the game:

Receptions: 8; 10

Receiving yards: 142; 155

Touchdowns: 1; 1

The 142 first-half yards were the most of Chase's career in a half, and the most by a Bengal in a first half since 1988. They were also more than any player had managed against Atlanta in a full game all season. He finished with 10 catches on 12 targets.

Burrow kept dealing

Joe Burrow completed 24 of 29 passes for 271 yards and three touchdowns, and ran in a fourth score himself. It was his fourth straight game with at least three touchdown passes, and his fifth in the last six games.

Tee Higgins caught five passes for 32 yards and scored twice, both on Burrow throws. Evan McPherson added a 57-yard field goal just before halftime and a 43-yarder late to close out the scoring.

The Bengals led 24-10 at halftime and scored on all four of their first-half possessions. They have now scored 30 points or more in four straight games, something the franchise hasn't done since 1997.

The Moss company

Chase's touchdown in Madrid put him alongside Randy Moss as the only receivers in NFL history with seven or more touchdowns in each of their first six seasons.

That's the kind of company that says everything about a career, not just a game. Moss did it across his first six years in Minnesota. Chase is doing it in an era where the Bengals' offense runs through him and Burrow — and on Sunday, in front of a Madrid crowd that had never seen an NFL game before, he made it look easy.

Atlanta's side

The Falcons fell to 3-6. Bijan Robinson ran for 147 yards, and Atlanta ran for 157 as a team, but the Falcons couldn't keep pace once Cincinnati's offense got rolling. Two pass interference flags on cornerback DJ Turner II set up both Atlanta touchdowns — the only real blemish on a Bengals defensive day that ended with Barrett Carter's diving interception sealing the win.

Atlanta had scored on its first two drives. After that, Cincinnati's offense simply outscored them, and the Falcons never got closer than seven points again.

What it means

The Bengals host the Steelers next Sunday night at Paycor Stadium, with first place in the AFC North on the line. Cincinnati is playing its best football of the season, and the offense that put up 34 points in Madrid has now done it four weeks running.

The Madrid game was billed as a showcase. Chase turned it into a statement.

Watch

Watch the full game highlights: Bengals vs. Falcons Week 9 | Nov 8, 2026 — NFL. The full game highlights from the Bengals' 34-17 win over the Falcons in Madrid, including Chase's 47-yard touchdown and Burrow's four total scores.

TSL Bets · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:04 AM ET

TSL Bets: SNF slip — Williams under 250.5, Loveland TD, Swift under 65.5 (about +790)

By Matt

The Sunday night slip is a game-script bet: Caleb Williams under 250.5 passing yards (-125) against the league's ninth-ranked pass defense, Colston Loveland to score (+165), and D'Andre Swift under 65.5 rushing yards (-115). Three legs, about +790.

The game

Tampa Bay Buccaneers (3-5) at Chicago Bears (6-2) — 8:20 PM ET, Soldier Field, NBC

Bears -6, total 47.5, moneyline Bears -270 / Buccaneers +220 (optimal-bet, DraftKings). The Bears have won three straight since Caleb Williams returned from injury — the last two against last season's Super Bowl teams, including a 34-17 win over the defending champions in Seattle on Monday night. Chicago is 6-2 against the spread and averages 387.3 total yards per game. Tampa Bay averages 300.0 total yards per game and relies on the ground game, with 168.6 rushing yards per contest against just 147.5 passing yards.

Injuries: Tampa Bay's Emeka Egbuka is good to go after practicing all week, and Bucky Irving is healthy — he ran for 170 yards last week against Atlanta. Baker Mayfield is having a miserable year by his standards: a career-worst 5.9 adjusted yards per attempt and just three touchdown passes across five games. On the Chicago side, Williams, Swift, and Loveland are all active with no injury designation reported this week — Williams missed three games earlier this season with a hamstring injury but has been hot since returning.

Storylines: Williams is 70.9% completions with six touchdowns and one interception, averaging 253.4 yards per game and 8.5 adjusted yards per attempt — legitimate MVP buzz when he is on the field. But Tampa Bay's pass defense ranks ninth in the league at 205.9 yards allowed per game, and the Bucs lead the league in time of possession with a run-heavy approach (53.5% handoffs over the last four games). The Bears are 6-point favorites at home on Sunday night, and the market expects Chicago to win — the question is how.

The legs

Leg 1 — Caleb Williams under 250.5 passing yards (-125)

Shop for the 250.5 — the consensus line is 245.5 at DraftKings and FanDuel, 246.5 at bet365, and 248.5 at BetMGM, but 250.5 is available at some books. Williams is hot: 305, 308, and 247 yards in his last three games, and he has thrown for 247 or more in each of his last six night games against NFC opponents. That is the honest caveat, and it is a real one. But the game script points under: Tampa Bay's pass defense is ninth in the league, the Bucs chew clock with the run and lead the league in time of possession, and the Bears are 6-point favorites who can win this game on the ground. OPTIMAL projects Williams at 245.7 yards. This is a game-script bet, not a talent bet — Williams is great, and the conditions still point under.

Leg 2 — Colston Loveland anytime touchdown (+165, BetMGM)

Loveland at plus money is the value leg. He is Williams' red-zone security blanket, and the Bucs' defense has been vulnerable to tight ends in the red zone all season. At +165, this is the leg that turns a solid slip into a +790 slip. If the Bears score through the air, Loveland is the most likely name on the scoresheet after the receivers — and the price says the market has not caught up to his role yet.

Leg 3 — D'Andre Swift under 65.5 rushing yards (-115, BetMGM)

Swift has 545 rushing yards and 10 rushing touchdowns in eight games, but the volume and efficiency tell a different story than the touchdown total. His game log: 124, 45, 84, 58, 43, 44, 78, 69 — under 65.5 in five of eight games. He is averaging 3.8 yards per carry, and last week against Seattle he needed 27 carries to get 69 yards. The Bears are favorites who will run the ball, but Swift's share of that work is not as big as the rushing total suggests, and the Bucs' run defense has been stout enough to hold him under this number.

Why this slip

Three legs, one game, one story: the Bears win a game that stays under the number. Williams throws for fewer than 251 yards because Tampa Bay controls the clock and the Bears lean on the run. Swift stays under 65.5 because his efficiency is poor and the workload is shared. Loveland scores because the Bears still need to finish drives, and he is the red-zone target. The legs fit together — if the game goes the way the slip needs it to, all three hit.

The honest caveat

Williams is the risk. He has thrown for 247 or more in six straight night games against NFC opponents, he is coming off 305 and 308, and he is playing at an MVP level. If he gets hot early, the under is dead by halftime. Loveland at +165 is plus money for a reason — tight end touchdowns are the most volatile prop on the board. And Swift has 10 rushing touchdowns in eight games; if he finds the end zone early, the Bears may feed him the ball. The slip pays about +790 because the Williams under is a bet against a quarterback who is playing the best football of his life.

The pick

3-leg SNF slip, about +790: Caleb Williams under 250.5 passing yards (-125) + Colston Loveland anytime TD (+165) + D'Andre Swift under 65.5 rushing yards (-115). A $10 ticket returns about $89.20. A game-script bet on Sunday night: the Bears win, the clock runs, and the numbers stay under. That is the bet.

TSL Bets · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:04 AM ET

TSL Bets parlay: Higgins over 73.5, Lions -6.5, Broncos -2.5 (about +560)

By Matt

Three legs, about +560: Tee Higgins over 73.5 receiving yards in Madrid (-122), the Lions -6.5 in Miami (-110), and the Broncos -2.5 in Carolina (-110).

The games

Cincinnati Bengals (5-2) at Atlanta Falcons (3-5) — 9:30 AM ET, Santiago Bernabéu, Madrid, NFL Network

Bengals -3.5, total 52.5, moneyline Bengals -163 / Falcons +160 (optimal-bet). The highest total on the board this week. Injuries: Atlanta is without Brian Robinson Jr. (hamstring) and Zachariah Branch (hamstring). Cincinnati is without safety Kyle Dugger (quad). Storylines: the Bengals gave up 36 points to a Titans team that had been averaging 14.5 a game, and the Falcons lost 16-0 to the Buccaneers in a game played through heavy rain. Both teams are coming off ugly weeks, and the total of 52.5 says the market expects points in Madrid.

Detroit Lions (5-2) at Miami Dolphins (1-6) — 1:00 PM ET, Hard Rock Stadium

Lions -6.5 (-110), total 49.5, moneyline Lions -310 / Dolphins +250 (bet365). Detroit scores the third-most points in the league at 29.0 per game and ranks eighth in offensive DVOA. Miami allows the ninth-most points at 25.9 per game. Injuries: Detroit is without Jack Campbell (neck, IR), Cade Mays (ankle), D.J. Reed (hip), Kerby Joseph (knee), and D.J. Wonnum (concussion). Amon-Ra St. Brown (ribs) is questionable but is good to go. Miami's questionable list is mostly depth: Kaytron Allen (quad), Jaylen Wright (quad), and Malik Herring (neck). Storylines: the Lions' offense against one of the league's worst defenses, and a Dolphins team that has covered three of its last four — but covering and winning are different things.

Denver Broncos (6-2) at Carolina Panthers (3-4) — 1:00 PM ET, Bank of America Stadium, CBS

Broncos -2.5 (-120), total 44.5, moneyline Broncos -146 / Panthers +124 (FanDuel via Sportsbook Wire). Denver is 6-2 against the spread this season. Injuries: Denver is without Kris Abrams-Draine (ankle), Brandon Jones (hamstring), and Eyioma Uwazurike (shoulder), with Talanoa Hufanga (ankle) questionable. Carolina's offensive line is the story — Taylor Moton is on IR, Ikem Ekwonu is on PUP, Damien Lewis (knee), Jaelan Phillips (hamstring), Bobby Okereke (foot), and Will Lee III (hamstring) are all out. Bryce Young is on the injury report with an ankle issue but was a full participant. Storylines: Carolina's offense managed just 266 and 258 total yards in its last two losses, Young was sacked six times at Green Bay, and Denver's pass rush (2.9 sacks per game) faces a line that allows 3.3 sacks per game, the second-most in the league. The Broncos have won and covered both of their road games this season.

The legs

Leg 1 — Tee Higgins over 73.5 receiving yards (-122, BetRivers)

Higgins has 43 catches for 726 yards and four touchdowns in seven games. He had 10 catches for 155 yards last week against Tennessee, and 303 yards in the two games since the bye. He has topped 90 yards in all but two games this season. The Falcons rank 24th in DVOA against No. 2 wide receivers and allow 148.2 yards per game to the position. This is the highest total on the board, in a dome-like atmosphere in Madrid, against a defense that gives up yards to receivers. Higgins over 73.5 is the cleanest receiving prop on the slate.

Leg 2 — Detroit Lions -6.5 (-110)

The Lions score 29 points a game. The Dolphins give up 25.9. Detroit is the better team by a touchdown on paper, and the spread is 6.5. Miami has covered three of its last four, but the Lions' offense is the kind that turns a cover into a blowout — and the Dolphins' injury list is mostly on the defensive side of the ball. This is the chalk leg, and it is chalk for a reason.

Leg 3 — Denver Broncos -2.5 (-110)

The Broncos are 6-2 against the spread, they have won and covered both road games, and they are playing a Panthers team whose offensive line is held together with tape and whose quarterback was sacked six times last week. Denver's pass rush against Carolina's line is the biggest mismatch in this game, and a short road number of 2.5 does not reflect how far apart these teams are right now. Covers' top pick is the Broncos.

Why these three together

Three different games, three different kinds of bet, one story: the better team, the better receiver, and the better matchup, all at fair prices. The parlay pays about +560 because each leg is a real bet on its own, and putting them together is how you get paid for being right three times.

The honest caveat

Parlays multiply risk, not just payout. Higgins has to clear 73.5 against a Falcons defense that, for all its flaws, just shut out Tampa Bay — even if that was in the rain. The Lions are laying 6.5 on the road against a Dolphins team that has covered three of four, and Detroit's defense is missing four starters. The Broncos are laying points on the road with a banged-up secondary of their own. Any one of these legs can lose on its own. That is why the price is +560 and not -560. Bet it as a fun ticket on three good reads, not as a mortgage payment.

The pick

3-leg parlay, about +560: Tee Higgins over 73.5 receiving yards (-122) + Detroit Lions -6.5 (-110) + Denver Broncos -2.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $66.30. A morning prop in Madrid, a road favorite in Miami, and a road favorite in Carolina. That is the bet.

TSL Bets: 4pm window slip — JSN TD, Bowers TD, Texans -5.5 (about +725)

By Matt

The 4pm window slip is the stars-and-chalk slip: Jaxon Smith-Njigba to score (-125) against the league's leakiest pass defense, Brock Bowers to score (+140) against a banged-up 49ers secondary, and the Texans -5.5 (-110) against a winless Chargers team. Three legs, about +725.

The games

Arizona Cardinals (3-5) at Seattle Seahawks (4-4) — 4:25 PM ET, Lumen Field, FOX

Storylines: Jaxon Smith-Njigba leads the NFL in receiving yards and receiving touchdowns; the Cardinals have allowed 18 passing touchdowns (tied for the most in the league) and 29 total touchdowns through eight games (the most); and Lock is 72.9% completions with four touchdowns and zero interceptions this season.

Las Vegas Raiders (4-4) at San Francisco 49ers (6-1) — 4:25 PM ET, Levi's Stadium, FOX

Storylines: the 49ers are 9.5-point favorites with their top three receivers out; the Raiders' offense is the healthiest unit on the field; and the total of 52.5 says the market expects points even with San Francisco's injuries.

Houston Texans (2-5) at Los Angeles Chargers (0-7) — 4:05 PM ET, SoFi Stadium, CBS

Texans -5.5 (-110), total 40.5, moneyline Texans -235 / Chargers +200 (Win Daily, FanDuel/BetMGM). The Chargers opened as 2.5-point favorites; the line has reversed all the way to Houston -6 at some books. Injuries: Houston is without David Montgomery (groin) and Reed Blankenship (concussion/hand). Los Angeles is without Derwin James Jr. (hamstring), Rashawn Slater (ankle), Deane Leonard (concussion), and Kendall Williamson (calf), with Brenen Thompson (quad/hamstring) and David Njoku (fibula) doubtful. Storylines: the Chargers are 0-7, score a league-worst 14.1 points per game, and Justin Herbert has five touchdowns against 10 interceptions while being sacked 23 times. C.J. Stroud has 11 touchdowns against one interception for 1,867 yards. Covers' top pick is Texans -5.5.

The legs

Leg 1 — Jaxon Smith-Njigba anytime touchdown (-125, DraftKings)

JSN leads the NFL with 985 receiving yards, 64 catches, and nine receiving touchdowns — the most in the league. Against Arizona specifically, with Lock at quarterback, he went 8-for-122-and-1 and 9-for-155-and-3 in Week 2. The Cardinals have allowed the most touchdowns in the NFL. Bowers at plus money is the value leg of the slip. Laying 5.5 on the road is never fun, but this is the right spot for it.

Why this slip

Two star-player touchdown legs at fair prices, plus a spread anchor on the most one-sided game of the window. JSN and Bowers are both the best red-zone options on their teams, and both are playing defenses that give up touchdowns. The Texans leg is the chalk that holds the slip together — it does not need to be pretty, it needs to be a win by a touchdown against a team that has not won a game all season.

And the Texans are laying 5.5 on the road, which is always a live number against a desperate team. The slip pays about +725 because all three legs have to hit, and the Bowers leg is a coin flip at best.

The pick

3-leg 4pm window slip, about +725: Jaxon Smith-Njigba anytime TD (-125) + Brock Bowers anytime TD (+140) + Houston Texans -5.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $82.50. The best receiver in football, the best tight end in football, and the worst team in football. That is the bet.

TSL Bets: 1pm window slip — Kyren TD, Puka over 92.5, Cowboys-Colts over 51.5 (about +500)

By Matt

The 1pm window slip leans on the Rams' run game in Washington and a shootout in Indianapolis: Kyren Williams to score (-145), Puka Nacua over 92.5 receiving yards (-114), and Cowboys-Colts over 51.5 (-110). Three legs, about +500.

The games

Los Angeles Rams (4-4) at Washington Commanders (2-4-1) — 1:00 PM ET, Northwest Stadium, FOX

Rams -4.5 (-110), total 47.5, moneyline Rams -235 / Commanders +191 (Action Network). The Rams have won two straight, 24-9 over the Chargers last week, and are 4-4 against the spread. Washington is coming off a convincing win over Philadelphia and is 4-3 ATS. The injury flag: Matthew Stafford was icing his shoulder after the Chargers win, and there is a chance he is not at 100%. That matters for the passing game — it does not matter much for the leg we are playing. On the Washington side, the defensive front is injury-depleted, which is exactly why the Rams' run game is the play. Storylines: the Rams' offense has found its identity on the ground, with Kyren Williams and Blake Corum becoming the keys to the attack; Washington's defense ranks as the second-leakiest in the league in passes to running backs relative to expectation; and the spread is close enough to keep Stafford honest, which keeps the Rams in scoring position all afternoon.

Dallas Cowboys (3-5) at Indianapolis Colts (2-6) — 1:00 PM ET, Lucas Oil Stadium, FOX

Cowboys -4.5, total 51.5, moneyline Cowboys -212 / Colts +208 (optimal-bet). Dallas is 5-2 to the over this season; Indianapolis is 4-3 to the over. The Colts have lost four straight, including a 42-7 loss to Jacksonville last week. Injuries: Dallas is without KaVontae Turpin (ribs) and Malachi Lawrence (ankle), and George Pickens (hamstring), Jake Ferguson (ankle), DeMarvion Overshown (hamstring/back), and Joey Porter Jr. (ankle) are all questionable. Indianapolis is without Akeem Davis-Gaither (knee) and Justin Walley (knee), with Jalen Travis (thumb) questionable and Cam Taylor-Britt (groin) on injured reserve. Storylines: both defenses are rotten — Dallas plays at the fastest pace in the league, the Colts have allowed 30 or more points in four games, and Indianapolis ranks 27th in defensive DVOA. The total has held at 51.5 all week, and the market expects points.

The legs

Leg 1 — Kyren Williams anytime touchdown (-145, Fanatics)

Williams has 112 carries for 548 yards and six touchdowns, averaging 4.9 yards per carry, and he has found the end zone in consecutive games. He is the most likely scorer in this game at Fanatics, and the matchup is right: Washington's defensive front is depleted, the Rams are 4.5-point favorites, and Williams is the goal-line back in an offense that is leaning on the run. The Stafford shoulder flag does not touch this leg — if anything, a limited Stafford means more Williams near the goal line.

Leg 2 — Puka Nacua over 92.5 receiving yards (-114, FanDuel)

Nacua is healthy with no injury designation, and he is the Rams' primary target with Davante Adams on injured reserve — 19 catches and two touchdowns in his last two games. The projection is roughly 9-10 receptions for about 96 yards. Washington's secondary has been leaky all season, and Nacua is the kind of receiver who gets there on volume alone. Even if Stafford is not at 100%, Nacua is the first read on nearly every dropback.

Leg 3 — Cowboys at Colts over 51.5 (-110)

Two bad defenses, one fast-paced offense, and a total that has not moved off 51.5. Dallas is 5-2 to the over this season. The Colts have allowed 30-plus in four games. Both teams can score — the question is whether either can stop the other, and the answer this season has been no. This is the shootout leg of the slip, and it is the one with the most margin for error: 52 points between two teams that both play fast and neither of which tackles well.

Why this slip

Two legs ride the Rams' offense in a game they should control, and the third rides a total that two bad defenses should clear. The legs are correlated in a good way — if the Rams score, Williams and Nacua are the two most likely reasons — but they are not the same bet three times. Williams needs one trip to the end zone. Nacua needs a big receiving day. The Cowboys and Colts need to do what they have done all season: score, and let the other team score.

The honest caveat

The Stafford shoulder is the real risk in this slip. If he is limited, the Rams' passing game shrinks, and Nacua's over gets harder — though Williams' leg gets easier. The Cowboys-Colts over is the other risk: 51.5 is a big number, and if either team has an off day, or if the questionable tags on Pickens and Ferguson turn into absences, the scoring dries up. And Williams at -145 is a short price for a reason — he has to actually get in. Nothing here is a lock. The slip pays about +500 because all three legs have to hit.

The pick

Gaming · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

World of Warcraft: Forever is the biggest launch in WoW history

By Matt

World of Warcraft: Forever launched November 4, and Blizzard says it is the biggest launch in the game's history — by peak concurrent players and returning players — with the most new players for a WoW launch in 15 years.

What happened

World of Warcraft: Forever is the biggest launch in World of Warcraft history.

Blizzard announced on Saturday, November 7, that the new version of WoW — which launched globally on Tuesday, November 4 at 3:00 p.m. PST, all regions at once — set records for peak concurrent players and for the highest number of returning players in the game's history. The company did not release exact figures, but the scale of the claim is the point: in a game that has been running for 22 years, this is the biggest launch it has ever had.

Executive producer Holly Longdale added another detail: Forever also drew the most new players for a WoW launch in 15 years. And in China, NetEase reported more than one million concurrent players — a record for the game in that region.

What Forever actually is

Forever is the third ongoing version of World of Warcraft, sitting alongside Retail and Classic. It is included in the existing subscription — no separate purchase — and it takes players back to the original 2004 version of Azeroth, with a level cap of 60, plus new zones, quests, dungeons, and raids built on top of that foundation.

The idea is the one Blizzard has been circling for years: the vanilla world, rebuilt with modern systems and new content, for players who want the old map without the old grind. The beta was the largest in franchise history, and the launch itself went smoothly — short queues, no major outages, which for a WoW launch is its own kind of news.

Why the numbers matter

WoW launches are usually measured in two ways: how many people show up, and how many of them stay. Blizzard is only claiming the first one so far — peak concurrents, returning players, new players — and it is not sharing the raw numbers. But the combination is notable: a record for returning players and the most new players in 15 years means Forever is pulling in both directions at once, luring back lapsed players and bringing in people who have never played.

The China number is the most concrete data point. One million concurrent players in a single region, on a single day, is a scale that very few games ever reach. NetEase's record suggests the launch was not just a Western nostalgia event.

What it means

World of Warcraft has been declared dead more times than any game in history, and it keeps answering with launches like this one. Forever is a bet that the original Azeroth — the map, the pace, the world — still has pull, and the first numbers say the bet is paying off. The real test is what happens in a month, when the launch rush fades and the question becomes whether players are still logging in. For now, though, Blizzard can say something it has never said before: this is the biggest launch the game has ever had.

The details

Launch: November 4, 2026, 3:00 p.m. PST, global

Record: Biggest launch in WoW history (peak concurrent players, returning players)

New players: Most for a WoW launch in 15 years (per Holly Longdale)

China: 1M+ concurrent players, a record (per NetEase)

Format: Third ongoing version, alongside Retail and Classic; included in subscription

Level cap: 60, original 2004 Azeroth + new zones, quests, dungeons, raids

Collectibles · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

Pokémon printed 10 billion cards a year — and still sold out

By Matt

The Pokémon Company printed roughly 10 billion cards in the fiscal year ending March 2026 — past 85 billion lifetime — and it still wasn't enough. Output has been flat for three years because the printers are already at maximum capacity, and the next big plant doesn't open until 2028.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Pokémon Company printed roughly 10 billion Pokémon TCG cards in the fiscal year ending March 31, 2026. The lifetime total is now past 85 billion cards since the game launched in 1996. The cards are printed in 16 languages and sold in more than 90 countries.

And it still was not enough. Shops are still selling out on day one.

That is the number at the center of the story: 10 billion sounds like a flood, but it is a ceiling. Output has been flat at around 10 billion a year for three years — the high-water mark was about 11.9 billion in fiscal 2023/24 — because the company has been printing at maximum capacity since January 2025. There is no more room on the presses. The only way to make more cards is to build more presses, and that takes years.

The sellout problem

The demand side is not a mystery. A London shop's allocation rose 40% and still sold out on day one, according to a Brit Brief report from a London Comic Con panel on November 6. That is the pattern everywhere: allocations go up, demand goes up faster, and the shelves are empty by the afternoon.

The timing makes it worse. Mega Evolution – Delta Reign and the 30th Celebration set launched on November 6, right into the holiday season, when demand is at its peak. New sets used to be a restock event. Now they are a race, and the race is over before most people get to the store.

The fix is coming — slowly

The Pokémon Company is building a new printing campus: 1.27 million square feet, due for completion in 2027, with full operation expected in late 2028. When it is running, it would roughly double the company's printing capacity.

That is a real fix, and it is also two years away. Between now and then, the math does not change: 10 billion cards a year, flat, against demand that keeps growing. The company is not holding back supply to create hype — it is printing as fast as it physically can, and it is still not enough.

What it means

For collectors, the takeaway is simple: the scarcity is structural, not artificial. The cards are not hard to find because the company is being cute with supply. They are hard to find because the printers are maxed out and the next plant is two years from full operation. That means day-one sellouts are the normal state of things until 2028, and the secondary market is where the action is — for better and for worse.

Ten billion cards a year. Eighty-five billion lifetime. And still sold out. That is the Pokémon economy right now.

Shop the drop

Looking for the latest Pokémon TCG releases? Pokémon TCG on Amazon

The numbers

Printed FY2025/26: ~10 billion cards

Lifetime total: Past 85 billion (since 1996)

Output trend: Flat ~10B/year for 3 years (high ~11.9B in FY23/24)

Capacity: Maximum since January 2025

Languages / countries: 16 languages, 90+ countries

New plant: 1.27M sq ft campus, completion 2027, full operation late 2028

Sports · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

Oregon scores 38 straight to stun Ohio State 41-28

By Matt

No. 23 Oregon trailed No. 12 Ohio State 14-0 in Columbus, then scored 38 unanswered points — sparked by a fake punt, a freshman interception, and a quarterback who wasn't supposed to be the starter — to win 41-28 and climb back into the Big Ten title race.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

No. 23 Oregon beat No. 12 Ohio State 41-28 on Saturday at Ohio Stadium, and the score does not tell you how absurd this game was.

The Ducks fell behind 14-0 in the first quarter. Ohio State's offense was rolling, the Horseshoe was loud, and Oregon looked like a team that was about to get run out of Columbus. Then the Ducks scored 38 consecutive points — four touchdowns on five drives, a fake punt, an interception, and a direct-snap touchdown as time expired in the first half — and by the time Ohio State scored again, more than two hours of game time had passed.

Quarterback Dylan Raiola finished 25 of 40 for 257 yards and two touchdowns, no interceptions. Dierre Hill Jr. ran 13 times for 75 yards and two touchdowns, including a 48-yard score to open the second half — the biggest run of Oregon's season. Dakorien Moore caught seven passes for 56 yards and a one-handed touchdown grab in the corner of the end zone. Brandon Smith added a 15-yard touchdown catch that made it 38-14.

The turning points

It is hard to say exactly when the game turned. Take your pick.

The fake punt: down 14-0, Oregon ran a fake on which Teitum Tuioti completed a pass to Matayo Uiagalelei for 30 yards. The drive ended in points, and the Ducks never looked back.

The interception: freshman safety Devin Jackson picked off Ohio State just before halftime, and Oregon turned it into a direct-snap touchdown from Jamari Johnson as time expired in the first half. The Ducks took the lead into the locker room.

The second half: Hill's 48-yard touchdown on the opening drive of the third quarter, then Moore's one-handed grab, then Smith's score. Ohio State's offense, meanwhile, managed 39 total yards in the second and third quarters — under 1.5 yards per play. The Buckeyes did not score for over two hours.

It was the first time Ohio State had allowed 38 consecutive points since 1999 against Wisconsin. The Buckeyes lost two straight games for the first time since 2011, and lost a third game in a regular season for the first time in 15 years.

The quarterback who wasn't supposed to be here

Raiola was not Oregon's quarterback to start the season. Dante Moore was. But Moore suffered a season-ending concussion in a 41-27 win over USC, Raiola struggled the following week in a loss to UCLA and was benched in the fourth quarter, and then — against Nebraska, the team Raiola had previously played for — he entered as the backup to Brock Thomas and rallied the Ducks to victory.

He has been Oregon's quarterback since. And on Saturday, in the biggest game of his season, he was the best player on the field.

"We were just confident in our plan," Raiola said. "We saw seams everywhere in their defense when we were game planning for them, and a couple of those first couple drives were self-inflicted wounds."

Head coach Dan Lanning, whose team is now 2-1 against the Buckeyes, put it more simply: "We're like cockroaches, man. We won't die."

Ohio State's side

Freshman Luke Fahey made his second career start for the injured Julian Sayin and threw for 360 yards and two touchdowns on 36-of-50 passing, with one interception. Jeremiah Smith caught a 13-yard touchdown pass. But the Buckeyes' offense vanished for two quarters, their defense gave up 395 yards to a Ducks attack led by a quarterback they could not contain, and the loss likely knocks Ohio State out of the College Football Playoff — and probably cost Smith the Heisman.

What it means

Oregon (6-3, 4-2 Big Ten) is back in the thick of the Big Ten title race and the playoff picture, with a second consecutive win in the Horseshoe — long considered one of college football's most intimidating stadiums. Ohio State (6-3, 4-2) is reeling, with two straight losses and a season that has slipped away in the span of a week.

The Ducks outgained the Buckeyes on the ground 108-53, won the turnover battle without giving the ball away once, and turned a 14-0 deficit into a 41-28 win that nobody saw coming — except, apparently, the quarterback who saw the seams all week.

The box score

Final score: Oregon 41, Ohio State 28

Raiola: 25-40, 257 yards, 2 TD, 0 INT

Hill: 13 carries, 75 yards, 2 TD

Moore: 7 catches, 56 yards, 1 TD

Fahey: 36-50, 360 yards, 2 TD, 1 INT

Records: Oregon 6-3 (4-2 Big Ten); Ohio State 6-3 (4-2 Big Ten)

Nikita Kucherov wins it in OT a week after his trade request went public

By Matt

A week after his trade request became public, Nikita Kucherov scored a power-play goal 3:23 into overtime to lift Tampa Bay past Minnesota 4-3 — and made it clear, again, that he intends to keep playing for the Lightning.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Tampa Bay Lightning beat the Minnesota Wild 4-3 in overtime on Saturday night in St. Paul, and the winner came from the one player everyone in the building was watching.

Nikita Kucherov scored a power-play goal 3:23 into overtime to win it for the Lightning, a week after his trade request became public. The Lightning improved to 11-3-2. The Wild fell to 12-3-4. But the score was almost beside the point — this was a night about Kucherov, and he answered the only way he knows how.

The trade request, in his words

The story broke on October 30, when Sportsnet's Elliotte Friedman reported that Kucherov had requested a trade. On November 2, Kucherov spoke publicly about it for the first time, and he was blunt about what it was — and what it was not.

"It was never about the money," Kucherov said. "Over the last four months, we only have had two conversations and Julien, in the end, made it clear that I'm free to walk on July 1."

The complaint, in his telling, was about "poor communication and respect" from the organization — not the contract, not the city, not the team. General manager Julien BriseBois responded that the Lightning "unequivocally have no intention of trading Nikita."

The numbers behind the standoff are enormous. Kucherov, 33, is the reigning Hart Trophy winner as NHL MVP, in the final year of an eight-year, $76 million deal ($9.5 million AAV). He reportedly sought around $20 million AAV over six years, per Chris Johnston, and submitted a 10-team trade list. He has also made it clear he intends to keep playing — and in his first game after the request went public, on October 31 at Nashville, he recorded three assists.

What it means

This is the part of the story that matters on the ice: Kucherov is still Kucherov. He is still producing, still the player the Lightning build their power play around, and still the one who ends games in overtime. The trade request is real, the frustration is real, and the contract standoff is real — but so is the production.

The Lightning have no intention of trading him. He has no intention of sitting out. And in the meantime, he keeps scoring overtime winners. That is the whole saga in one sentence, and it is not going away anytime soon.

The box score

Final score: Lightning 4, Wild 3 (OT)

Kucherov: OT power-play goal, 3:23 into overtime

Lightning: 11-3-2

Wild: 12-3-4

Trade request: Reported October 30 by Sportsnet's Elliotte Friedman

Contract: Final year of 8-year, $76M deal ($9.5M AAV)

NBA · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

Stephen Curry drops 41 as Warriors win seventh straight in OT over Magic

By Matt

Stephen Curry scored 41 points — including 12 unanswered in the final 2:21 of regulation — as the Warriors beat Orlando 126-119 in overtime for their seventh straight win. At 38, Curry just set the record for most 40-point games by a player 35 or older, and did something no player has done in the tracking era.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Golden State Warriors beat the Orlando Magic 126-119 in overtime on Saturday night at Chase Center, and Stephen Curry was the whole story — again.

Curry finished with 41 points, six rebounds, and six assists on 13-of-19 shooting, 5-of-10 from three, and a perfect 10-of-10 at the free-throw line. His three-point play with 1:38 left in overtime put the Warriors ahead for good, and his driving hoop 44 seconds later sealed his 41-point night. The Warriors won their seventh straight game. They started the season 0-3. They are now 7-3.

The finish

This game should not have been close. The Warriors led by 16 at halftime, and Orlando — short-handed, 2-7, on a losing streak — had no business being in it. But the Magic exploded for 37 points in the third quarter, and Jamal Cain's putback with two seconds left in regulation sent it to overtime.

Then Curry took over. He scored 12 unanswered points himself in the final 2:21 of regulation, and in the fourth quarter and overtime combined, he went a perfect 4-for-4 on go-ahead shots in the final two minutes. That is the first time in the tracking era — since the start of the 1996-97 season — that a player has made at least four field goals without a miss under those circumstances in a single game. All told, there were six different instances down the stretch when Curry made a shot to either tie the game or take the lead.

Wendell Carter Jr. missed two free throws with 1:15 left in overtime that would have tied it. Curry made sure Orlando never got another chance.

Why this one is historic

The 41 was Curry's second 40-point game this season, and his fourth in his last five games with at least 30 points. It was also his 16th 40-point game since turning 35 years old — breaking a tie with LeBron James for the most in NBA history by a player age 35 or older. Curry is 38.

And there was another milestone buried in the box score: Curry joined Oscar Robertson and Russell Westbrook as only the third point guard in NBA history to record 25,000 points and 5,000 rebounds in his career. He also passed 6,000 career assists, according to Elias.

The streak around him matters too. This is the Warriors' longest winning streak since seven in a row from March 3-15, 2025, and their first seven-game win streak without Jimmy Butler since January and February of their 2022 championship season. The team that started 0-3 and looked finished is now the hottest team in the West.

The help

Curry did not do it alone. Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg had 23 points, eight rebounds, and three blocks in his 10th career game — including a key three-pointer in overtime. Brandin Podziemski added 15 points and seven rebounds. Every Warriors player who logged minutes scored, and the bench outscored Orlando's second unit 42-31.

Orlando fought hard — 18 three-pointers, a 24-3 edge on the offensive boards, and Cain's 18 points and 12 rebounds off the bench — but it was the Magic's fourth straight loss, and Curry was the difference between a win and a loss.

What it means

There is a temptation to treat nights like this as routine for Curry. They are not routine. A 38-year-old point guard just went 4-for-4 on go-ahead shots in the final two minutes of regulation and overtime, set the record for 40-point games after 35, and joined two of the greatest point guards ever in a club that only had two members. The Warriors started 0-3. They have won seven straight. And the reason is the same as it has been for a decade: when the game gets tight, Stephen Curry decides it.

The box score

Final score: Warriors 126, Magic 119 (OT)

Curry: 41 pts, 6 reb, 6 ast, 13-19 FG, 5-10 3PT, 10-10 FT

Lendeborg: 23 pts, 8 reb, 3 blk

Podziemski: 15 pts, 7 reb

Warriors: 7-3, seven straight wins

Magic: 2-7, four straight losses

Johnny Walker TKOs Waldo Cortes-Acosta in chaotic UFC Vegas 124 main event

By Matt

Johnny Walker was knocked down in the first round and lost the first two rounds on the scorecards — then landed a question-mark kick, a backfist, and a right hook to become the first man ever to knock out Waldo Cortes-Acosta, stopping him in the third round at UFC Vegas 124.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Johnny Walker just pulled off the kind of comeback that makes heavyweight MMA the most unpredictable division in the sport.

Walker (24-10 MMA, 10-7 UFC) stopped Waldo Cortes-Acosta (17-5 MMA, 10-5 UFC) by TKO at 2:27 of the third round in the main event of UFC Vegas 124 (UFC Fight Night 293) on Saturday night at the Meta APEX in Las Vegas — becoming the first man ever to knock out Cortes-Acosta.

The finish came out of nowhere, which is exactly how Walker fights. After being knocked down in the first round and losing the first two rounds on MMA Junkie's scorecards (10-9, 10-9 Cortes-Acosta), Walker uncorked a question-mark kick, followed it with a backfist, and then landed the right hook that ended the fight. Cortes-Acosta, who had never been stopped by strikes before, went down and the fight was over.

A short-notice main event that delivered

This fight almost didn't happen — at least not like this. Walker took the main event on nine days' notice after the original headliner, Sean Brady vs. Michael Morales, was canceled when Brady suffered an injury. It was Walker's second heavyweight fight, and his second heavyweight win by knockout: he beat Mick Parkin by first-round KO in October.

That is the Johnny Walker experience in a nutshell. He is chaos in human form — a light heavyweight who moved up to heavyweight and has now finished both of his fights there by knockout, in a division where one clean shot ends everything. He was losing this fight. Clearly losing it. And then he wasn't.

Cortes-Acosta's loss is his third straight, after defeats to Alexander Volkov and Curtis Blaydes. The man who had never been knocked out now has that on his record, and the man who did it was supposed to be the short-notice replacement.

The co-main

In the co-main event, Tatiana Suarez beat Virna Jandiroba by unanimous decision (30-27, 30-27, 29-28), a strong performance in a strawweight fight with title implications. Suarez controlled the fight on the scorecards, and the win keeps her firmly in the mix at 115 pounds.

What it means

Walker is now 2-0 at heavyweight with two knockouts, and he did it the hard way — taking a fight on nine days' notice, getting dropped early, and finishing a man who had never been finished by strikes. Heavyweight is a division where momentum is everything, and Walker just grabbed a fistful of it. The question now is who the UFC matches him with next, because a heavyweight who can end a fight from anywhere, at any moment, is appointment viewing — even when he's losing.

The result

Winner: Johnny Walker (24-10 MMA, 10-7 UFC)

Loser: Waldo Cortes-Acosta (17-5 MMA, 10-5 UFC)

Method: TKO (punches), Round 3, 2:27

Event: UFC Vegas 124 (UFC Fight Night 293), Meta APEX, Las Vegas

Co-main: Tatiana Suarez def. Virna Jandiroba by unanimous decision (30-27, 30-27, 29-28)

NHL · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

Sidney Crosby passes Marcel Dionne for sixth all-time in NHL scoring

By Matt

Sidney Crosby scored twice in Pittsburgh's 5-2 win over Los Angeles, and his 1,772nd career point moved him past Marcel Dionne into sole possession of sixth place on the NHL's all-time scoring list. Ron Francis, in fifth, is 25 points away.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Pittsburgh Penguins beat the Los Angeles Kings 5-2 on Saturday night at Crypto.com Arena, snapping a four-game losing streak. The win was nice. The history was the story.

Sidney Crosby scored twice, and the first of those goals was his 1,772nd career point — moving him past Marcel Dionne (1,771) into sole possession of sixth place on the NHL's all-time scoring list. The milestone goal came at 17:13 of the second period, a Evgeni Malkin one-timer that deflected off Crosby and in. The empty-netter later in the game made it 1,773 points: 660 goals and 1,113 assists in 1,437 games.

Crosby tied Dionne at 1,771 in Montreal on October 31. It took him a week to pass him. He is 39 years old, and he is still doing this.

The list ahead of him

Crosby now sits sixth, behind only Wayne Gretzky, Jaromir Jagr, Mark Messier, Gordie Howe, and Ron Francis. The next name on the list is Francis, at 1,798 points — Crosby is 25 points shy of tying him, 26 from passing him. That is a chase that could realistically happen this season if Crosby stays healthy and keeps producing at his current rate: six goals and six assists for 12 points in 17 games so far.

The names around him tell you what kind of company this is. Dionne held sixth place for decades. Crosby just took it from him, and the only players left ahead of him are the ones whose names are on the building.

The rest of the night

It was not a one-man show. Malkin, whose one-timer set up the milestone goal, passed Adam Oates for 20th place on the all-time scoring list himself, with 1,421 career points. Tommy Novak scored twice as well. Sergei Murashov made 26 saves in goal as the Penguins ended their skid.

What it means

Crosby has been chasing history for so long that it is easy to forget how rare this is. Sixth all-time in scoring, in a league that has been around for more than a century, at 39, on a Penguins team that is rebuilding around him. The Francis chase is next, and it is close enough to watch in real time. After that, the mountain gets steep — Howe is at 1,850, and the top of the list is Gretzky's 2,857, which nobody is touching. But Crosby is not done climbing yet.

The box score

Final score: Penguins 5, Kings 2

Crosby: 2 goals, 1,773 career points (660 G, 1,113 A, 1,437 GP)

Milestone: Passed Marcel Dionne (1,771) for 6th all-time

Next target: Ron Francis, 1,798 (5th) — 25 points away

Malkin: Passed Adam Oates for 20th all-time (1,421 points)

Novak: 2 goals

Murashov: 26 saves

Penguins: Snapped a four-game losing streak

Sports · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

Notre Dame stuns No. 1 Miami 31-24 behind C.J. Carr's 319 yards

By Matt

C.J. Carr threw for 319 yards and three touchdowns as No. 11 Notre Dame knocked off No. 1 Miami 31-24 in South Bend — the Irish's first win over the nation's top-ranked team in six years, and their first over the Hurricanes in a decade.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

No. 11 Notre Dame beat No. 1 Miami 31-24 on Saturday night at Notre Dame Stadium, and it was not a fluke, a gift, or a game the Irish stole. They built a 24-3 lead, survived a furious Miami rally, and made the plays that mattered in the final four minutes to hand the Hurricanes their first loss of the season.

Quarterback C.J. Carr had the best game of his season — 29 of 40 for 319 yards and three touchdowns, no interceptions, spreading the ball to 10 different receivers. It was his first 300-yard game since a 36-7 win over N.C. State on October 11, 2025, and it came against the No. 1 team in the country with Notre Dame's playoff life on the line.

The Irish jumped out fast. Spencer Porath opened the scoring with a 44-yard field goal, and Carr found Jordan Faison for a 27-yard touchdown and Micah Gilbert for an 18-yarder just before halftime to make it 17-3. Gilbert's second touchdown — a 20-yarder to open the third quarter — pushed it to 24-3, and for a while it looked like a rout.

The comeback that almost was

Miami did not go quietly. Darian Mensah, the Heisman Trophy favorite, threw three touchdown passes between the third and fourth quarters — one to tight end Elija Lofton, two to Cooper Barkate — and suddenly the Hurricanes had the ball back down seven with a chance to tie.

The ending was chaos. With 3:36 left, Mensah appeared to hit Lofton for the game-tying touchdown, but officials ruled the tight end did not retain possession after bobbling the ball while landing both feet in the end zone. Notre Dame safety Luke Talich — who had already picked off Mensah for just the second interception of his season — knocked the ball loose on the fourth-down attempt. Miami's last gasp, a hook-and-lateral on the final play, ended in a fumble recovered by Jaiden Ausberry.

Mensah finished 24 of 43 for 276 yards and three touchdowns with the interception, plus 11 carries for 28 yards. He did it without his top target — star wideout Malachi Toney is out for an extended period after ankle surgery — and with Barkate stepping up for 10 catches and 92 yards. Miami's top-ranked offense managed just 74 passing yards and three points in the first half, and the Hurricanes were outgained 251-145 before the break.

Why this one matters

This was a statement game in the most literal sense. On Tuesday, the College Football Playoff committee ranked Notre Dame 11th — seven spots below its No. 4 AP ranking — and made it clear the Irish needed a win like this one to be taken seriously. They got it, and now they head into Week 11 in a near-lock position for the playoff.

The history is just as loud. It is Notre Dame's first victory over Miami in a decade, and the Irish are now 10-1 against the Hurricanes in South Bend and 19-9-1 in the series. It is Marcus Freeman's first victory over the AP's top-ranked team, and the 10th such win in program history (10-17-1 overall). And it came six years to the day after Notre Dame's most recent win over the nation's No. 1 team — the 47-40 double-overtime thriller over Clemson on November 7, 2020.

Gilbert finished with nine catches for 98 yards and two touchdowns. Faison added six catches for 77 yards and a score. Nolan James Jr. ran 16 times for 52 yards and a one-yard touchdown in the fourth quarter, set up by Mensah's interception. Miami head coach Mario Cristobal told reporters afterward he was "beyond upset" about the incomplete ruling on Lofton's would-be tying score — which tells you how close this one was.

What it means

Miami (8-1, 6-0 ACC) still has a clear path to the playoff — it was the No. 1 overall seed in last week's CFP rankings, and a season-ending three-game homestand starts next week against Duke. But the Hurricanes' margin for error is gone, and they will be doing it without Toney.

Notre Dame (8-1) is the story. The Irish went from committee afterthought to playoff near-lock in one night, and they did it the way Freeman's teams are supposed to do it: a quarterback playing the game of his life, a defense that bent and then broke Miami's last two drives, and a stadium that got louder as the night went on. Next up: Boston College, back in South Bend, Saturday at 3:30 p.m. ET.

The box score

C.J. Carr: 29-40, 319 yards, 3 TD, 0 INT

Micah Gilbert: 9 catches, 98 yards, 2 TD

Jordan Faison: 6 catches, 77 yards, 1 TD

Nolan James Jr.: 16 carries, 52 yards, 1 TD

Darian Mensah: 24-43, 276 yards, 3 TD, 1 INT

Cooper Barkate: 10 catches, 92 yards

Final score: Notre Dame 31, Miami 24

Records: Notre Dame 8-1; Miami 8-1 (6-0 ACC)

NBA · Nov 7, 2026 · 5:08 PM ET

Celtics set NBA Cup record with 24 threes

By Matt

Jayson Tatum dropped 34 points in 29 minutes and Boston buried an NBA Cup-record 24 threes in a 119-80 rout of the Hornets — a wire-to-wire blowout that pushed the Celtics to 7-2 and 2-0 in Cup play.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Boston Celtics routed the Charlotte Hornets 119-80 on Friday night at Spectrum Center, and the score barely tells the story. Boston led 40-20 after the first quarter, by 31 at halftime, and by as many as 46 points on the way to its second NBA Cup group-play win.

Jayson Tatum scored a game-high 34 points in just 29 minutes — he did not play a minute in the fourth quarter — and the Celtics made 24 three-pointers on 54.5% shooting from deep. That is a new NBA Cup record.

The win moved Boston to 7-2 on the season and 2-0 in East Group C, where the Celtics now lead the group by record and own a +53 scoring differential — 44 points clear of the next team.

The three-point blitz

The record was not a late-game accident. Boston hit 17 threes in the first half alone, tying a franchise record, and kept firing until the 24th fell. The Celtics finished 24-of-44 from deep (54.5%) with 33 assists as a team.

They were even flirting with bigger history. The NBA record for most threes in a game is 29 — a mark the Celtics themselves tied on Opening Night in 2024 against the Knicks. They settled for the Cup record instead, and with the game long decided, there was no reason to chase it.

The contrast on the other end was stark. Charlotte shot 11-of-50 from three (22%) and 27-of-92 from the field overall (29.3%) in its second-lowest scoring performance of the season. The loss was the Hornets' sixth straight, dropping them to 3-7.

Tatum's night

Tatum did most of his damage before halftime: 24 of his 34 points came in the first two quarters, including 18 in the first. He finished 9-of-16 from the field, 5-of-8 from three, and a perfect 11-of-11 from the free-throw line, with 8 rebounds, 9 assists, a steal, and a team-best plus-36.

The road crowd in Charlotte even gave him MVP chants — fitting for a player who averages nearly 30 points per game against the Hornets, the most he averages against any of the 29 other teams.

It is also worth remembering how recently this version of Tatum was in doubt. He returned from a torn Achilles tendon on March 6 of last season, and nights like this one — efficient, unhurried, done by the fourth quarter — are the clearest sign yet that the explosion and the shot-making are fully back.

He had help. Derrick White added 18 points, including 10 in the first quarter, with 4 threes, 5 assists, and 4 rebounds. Payton Pritchard came off the bench for 12 points and 4 more threes. Tatum was the only player on either side to reach 20 points.

The box score

Final score: Celtics 119, Hornets 80

Celtics threes: 24-44 (54.5%) — NBA Cup record

Hornets threes: 11-50 (22.0%)

Tatum: 34 PTS, 8 REB, 9 AST, 9-16 FG, 5-8 3PT, 11-11 FT, +36

White: 18 PTS, 4 REB, 5 AST, 4-6 3PT

Pritchard: 12 PTS, 4-7 3PT

Team assists: 33

Celtics record: 7-2 (2-0 in NBA Cup group play)

Hornets record: 3-7 (sixth straight loss)

Around the game

• Brandon Miller: Charlotte's night got worse when Miller left the game with a groin injury.

• First stop on the road: the game opened a four-game road trip for Boston.

• Cup math: point differential matters in NBA Cup group play, and Boston's +53 through two games is the best in East Group C by 44 points.

• Next Cup game: Boston's next group-play game is not until Tuesday, Nov. 24, against the Wizards.

What it means

This was a statement in the exact form the NBA Cup rewards: win big, and the tiebreaker takes care of itself. The Celtics did not just beat an injury-riddled, struggling Hornets team — they buried them from three, sat their star for the fourth quarter, and still walked out with the best point differential in their group.

The bigger signal is Tatum. A 34-point night in 29 minutes, with the efficiency and the bounce, against a team he has always torched, says the Celtics' best player is rounding into form at the same time the team is stacking wins. Boston's next test is a real one: at Milwaukee on Sunday night.

What's next

Boston visits the Milwaukee Bucks on Sunday, Nov. 8, at 8 p.m. at Fiserv Forum.

MLB · Nov 7, 2026 · 2:08 PM ET

Red Sox predicted to sign Freddy Peralta for $92M

By Matt

Sports Illustrated's Nicholas Selbe predicts Boston lands Freddy Peralta on a four-year, $92 million deal — a bet on the ace's Tampa turnaround after Sonny Gray elected free agency.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The prediction

The Red Sox need a starter, and one of the offseason's biggest names is being linked to Boston. Sports Illustrated's Nicholas Selbe predicted the Red Sox will sign Freddy Peralta to a four-year, $92 million contract this offseason.

The fit is obvious. Sonny Gray has elected free agency, leaving a hole at the top of Boston's rotation. Peralta is a two-time All-Star and one of the top arms on the open market — and after a wild 2026, his value is trending back up at exactly the right time.

The Jekyll-and-Hyde season

Peralta's 2026 was a tale of two teams. He opened the year with the New York Mets, where it went sideways: a 5-9 record and a 4.99 ERA in 22 appearances. A deadline trade to Tampa Bay changed everything.

With the Rays, Peralta went 5-2 with a 3.25 ERA in 10 appearances, allowing just three earned runs over his final six regular-season starts — a 0.79 ERA down the stretch. Overall for the season, he finished 10-11 with a 4.42 ERA and 156 strikeouts in 32 games.

Then came October. Peralta was named ALCS MVP after posting a 0.75 ERA over three appearances (12 innings) against Cleveland, helping the Rays reach the World Series against his old club, Milwaukee.

New York Mets: 5-9; 4.99; —

Tampa Bay Rays: 5-2; 3.25; —

Full season: 10-11; 4.42; 156

Why Boston believes in the Tampa version

The Rays version of Peralta is the one Boston would be paying for — and it's not hard to see why. A year earlier, in his final season with the Brewers, Peralta went 17-6 with a 2.70 ERA and 204 strikeouts, finishing fifth in NL Cy Young voting. That is the track record underneath the messy Mets half-season: a durable, strikeout-heavy starter with 30 starts in four consecutive years.

The question for Boston is which version shows up in 2027. The case for the Red Sox: the Tampa turnaround wasn't a small sample blip. It was a full second-half reset — a 3.25 ERA, a dominant closing stretch, and an ALCS MVP — from a 30-year-old entering his age-31 season who has done this before.

The competition

Boston won't be alone in the bidding. MLB Trade Rumors' Steve Adams predicted Peralta lands with the Chicago Cubs on a five-year, $110 million deal, and the Blue Jays, Giants, Padres, White Sox, and Orioles have all been named as fits. Spotrac has floated a seven-year, $201 million projection, while The Athletic's Jim Bowden predicted five years and $140 million.

Selbe's $92 million over four years would be the shorter, cheaper end of that range — a bet that Boston can land the ace without going to the nine-figure lengths other suitors might. Whether Peralta takes a shorter deal or chases the longer one is the open question of his winter.

What it would mean for Boston

If the prediction holds, the Red Sox would be buying a proven top-of-the-rotation arm to replace Gray — and keeping him away from the rest of the AL East in the process. The Rays aren't expected to re-sign him at this price, and every team in the division knows what the Tampa version of Peralta looks like. Boston landing him would be a statement that the rotation hole gets filled with a real ace, not a stopgap.

TSL Bets · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

2-Leg Parlay (about +257): Spurs -5.5 + Canadiens Moneyline

By Matt

Two plays, one story: the league's hottest big man against a shorthanded backcourt, and the East's hottest team at a coin-flip price. Together they pay about +257.

The legs

Leg 1 — San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110)

San Antonio Spurs at Portland Trail Blazers, 10:00 PM ET. Portland is down Ja Morant, Shaedon Sharpe, and Vit Krejci on the second night of a back-to-back, and Victor Wembanyama has scored 82 points in his last two games — 52 against Dallas on Wednesday, 30 against Sacramento on Friday. The Blazers just spent Friday night chasing the Lakers around Los Angeles. The Spurs are the better, healthier team, and the line has already moved from -4.5 to -5.5.

Leg 2 — Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115)

Montreal Canadiens at Toronto Maple Leafs, 7:00 PM ET. The Canadiens are 10-3-3, have won six of seven and four straight over Toronto, and start Jacob Fowler (7-1-1, .925 save percentage). The Leafs have lost three of four and are held below 30 shots a night lately. Tavares is expected back for Toronto, but one player returning from three weeks out doesn't close the gap at a coin-flip price.

Why these two together

Both legs are bets on the better, healthier team — and both are priced like coin flips. The Spurs need to beat a shorthanded team by six. The Canadiens need to beat a slumping team straight up. Neither asks for a miracle. Put them together and the price jumps to about +257 — a $10 ticket returns about $35.70 — which is the whole point: two solid reads, one ticket.

The honest caveat

Parlays multiply risk, not just payout. The Spurs are laying points on the road on a back-to-back, and Wembanyama rolled his ankle Friday — he finished the game, but if he is limited, leg one is in trouble. The Canadiens are the better team, but the Leafs are rested, at home, on a Saturday night, with their captain back. Either leg can lose on its own. That is why the price is +257 and not -257. Bet it as a fun ticket on two good reads, not as a mortgage payment.

The pick

2-leg parlay, about +257: San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110) + Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115). The hottest big man in the league and the hottest team in the East, both at fair prices. That is the bet.

TSL Bets · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

Canadiens Moneyline (-115): The East's hottest team against a slumping rival — even with Tavares back

By Matt

Montreal is 10-3-3, has won six of seven and four straight over Toronto, and starts a goalie at 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage. The Leafs get Tavares back, but -115 is still the value side.

Game slip

Montreal Canadiens (10-3-3) at Toronto Maple Leafs (8-7-2)

Saturday, November 7, 2026 — 7:00 PM ET — Scotiabank Arena, Toronto, ON — ESPN+, SN, TVAS

Total: 6.5

Moneyline: Canadiens -115 / Maple Leafs +105

Odds source: ESPN via DraftKings, Nov. 7

### Injury report

Montreal: RW Ivan Demidov (ankle) — injured reserve. D Mike Matheson (lower body) — out. G Samuel Montembeault (lower body) — injured reserve, day-to-day, could return after a month out. D Arber Xhekaj (undisclosed) — questionable. Expected starter Jacob Fowler is 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage and a 2.17 goals-against average.

Toronto: C John Tavares (lower body) — expected to return after missing nine games since October 15. He practiced Friday in a regular jersey, skated on a line with Nick Paul and William Nylander, and rejoined the top power-play unit. RW Kirill Marchenko (lower body) — injured reserve. C Max Domi (back) — injured reserve. F Miles Wood — injured reserve. D Nick Blankenburg — injured reserve. G Elvis Merzlikins (shoulder) — injured reserve.

### Storylines

• Montreal is the hottest team in the East right now: 10-3-3, winners of six of seven, coming off a 5-4 overtime win over Utah on Thursday in which Nick Suzuki returned from a six-game absence with three points and Cole Caufield scored the winner 30 seconds into OT.

• The Canadiens carry a four-game win streak over the Maple Leafs into Scotiabank Arena, and they just showed they can win the hard way — rallying from 2-0 and 4-3 down on Thursday, two nights after blowing a 4-0 lead to Winnipeg.

• Toronto is slumping: 8-7-2, losers of three of four, held below 30 shots in four straight games, and 4-4-1 at home. The middle six has buckled with Tavares and Marchenko out, and the Leafs are starting a stretch of five straight games against teams in a playoff position.

• The goalie matchup tilts Montreal's way. Fowler is 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage; one starting-goalie service projects Sergei Bobrovsky for Toronto at .889 with a 3.27 goals-against average. That is a big gap in a game lined as a coin flip.

The bet

Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115)

This is a bet on the better team at a coin-flip price. Montreal is better at even strength, better in goal, deeper down the lineup, and has owned this matchup lately — four straight wins over Toronto. The Leafs' best argument is Tavares returning and three days of rest, and both are real. But Tavares is one player coming back from a three-week absence, likely on the wing for the first time, and rest doesn't fix a team that has been outshot and outworked for a week. At -115, you're getting the 10-3-3 team at the price of a toss-up. Take it.

Why

The market is pricing the name on the front of the jersey, not the team on the ice. Toronto at home, on Hockey Night in Canada, against Montreal — that matchup always draws Leafs money, and it has kept this line near even despite everything in the form guide pointing the other way. Montreal has the better record by a distance, the better goalie by a distance, the head-to-head streak, and the momentum of a team that just stared down a brutal loss and answered with a comeback win. The Habs' blue line is banged up, but Fowler has covered for it all season.

The honest caveat

Tavares matters. He had seven points in seven games before the injury, he is a point-per-game player for his career, and his return immediately fixes Toronto's top power play and its faceoff circle. The Leafs have also had three days off since their 3-2 loss to Utah on November 3, while Montreal played Thursday and had one day of rest. And Montreal is missing Demidov, Matheson, and possibly Montembeault — if Fowler has an off night, there is no safety net. A rested Leafs team with its captain back, at home, on a Saturday night, is a live underdog. The price is fair, not a steal.

The pick

Canadiens moneyline (-115). The better team, the better goalie, the better form — at a coin-flip price.
