Gaming · Nov 4, 2026 · 1:11 PM ET

Dead by Daylight Removes TheBurntPeanut's Free Content in a Second, Longer Statement

By Matt

Behaviour Interactive has removed the Dead by Daylight content gifted to streamer TheBurntPeanut, saying it moved too fast and broke trust — and its second statement, posted November 3, lays out exactly what it says went wrong and what changes now.

Dead by Daylight's Halloween-weekend controversy has a second chapter. Behaviour Interactive has now removed the in-game content it gifted to Twitch streamer TheBurntPeanut, and its longer follow-up statement — posted November 3 on the main Dead by Daylight account — admits the studio moved too fast, skipped its own checks, and broke trust with its community.

This is a follow-up to our November 2 post on the first apology. That first statement, posted November 1 on the smaller DbD Pulse account, said the gift was made without appropriate approvals. The new statement, a four-page post, is Behaviour's attempt to answer the questions players were actually asking.

What happened

On October 30, during a Halloween stream, TheBurntPeanut played Dead by Daylight for the first time. Members of Behaviour's team noticed a very large audience in the game's Twitch directory watching a streamer who had not played it before, and saw a chance to welcome new players.

A staff member then gifted TheBurntPeanut access to the game's DLC characters, perks, and in-game currency during the livestream. When TheBurntPeanut offered to give the content back, concerned about the backlash to come, the official account replied in stream chat: "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT! THEY ARE YOURS TO KEEP!"

The backlash that followed had two layers. Critics raised concerns about Behaviour partnering with TheBurntPeanut, pointing to clips from his past streams, and many players objected to the dismissive tone of the official account's reply. Some players said they uninstalled the game as the dispute escalated.

What Behaviour says it got wrong

The November 3 statement breaks the failure into three parts, in the studio's own framing:

Due diligence. The decision to give the creator in-game content and join his chat was made before taking the necessary time to look at whether it was a good idea. It happened quickly and outside the team's usual discussions. Behaviour says it normally looks thoroughly at a new creator's past content to judge whether they represent the values of inclusion its community expects. In this case, it says, that was not taken into proper consideration or discussed as a group.

Internal process. The studio says it did not have stringent enough processes and guardrails to prevent in-game items from being granted directly to players without larger consensus and approval. A team member acted without a supporting system behind them to guide them to another outcome.

Communication standards. The "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT!" reply, along with some other messages in that session, showed a disregard for the context the team was suddenly in and crossed the line of appropriate responses from the brand, Behaviour says. That reply escalated the situation for the studio and for the community, especially for content creators on the frontlines of those conversations, and that was unacceptable.

Behaviour also states its values in the post: respect, transparency, diversity, and creating a space where people from different backgrounds and experiences feel welcome. Diversity is one of Dead by Daylight's core principles, it says, and when it chooses to work with a creator, it is putting its name next to theirs.

What is changing now

• The content is gone. Behaviour says it has already removed the content that was granted to the creator.

• Creator guidelines are being rewritten. That includes how the team interacts with creators playing Dead by Daylight, who on the team can make those calls, and how to identify creators whose past conduct is in direct opposition to the inclusive community Behaviour says it is trying to build.

• Approval methods are being updated. Request and approval methods for granting content and currency to players are being reworked, with checks and balances at the right time, and the team will be trained on the new processes.

• Creator Program rules are being enforced directly. Creators found to have violated Creator Program policy will result in removal. Any hold or removal will be communicated directly to the creator involved, with no additional communications on social channels or in program Discords. All removals are at the team's discretion.

• Internal action is confidential. Behaviour says it takes the situation very seriously and is taking appropriate action internally, but as a confidential matter it will not share further details.

The statement closes with a direct admission: "This incident has broken trust with our community who hold expectations and regards for the types of people we partner with, but more importantly expectations of the conduct and actions taken by our team in our daily work within this community. We understand it will be a slow road to regain that trust but we are committed to doing the work necessary to get there."

The response, by the numbers

The November 3 statement post itself drew a large reaction on X, with 11K comments, 4.4K reposts, and 28K likes at the time of the screenshots shared for this story — a measure of how widely this one is still being watched, not a verdict in itself.

TheBurntPeanut has also responded on stream, saying he saw the reaction online and noting a refund request that cited the collaboration and the communication team's response. He said he did not think he had said anything about transgender people in the last two to two-and-a-half years of streaming, and asked whether he ever had. That response is his, not Behaviour's, and the underlying allegations from critics remain allegations about past clips, not findings in Behaviour's statement — which focuses on its own process, not on adjudicating the streamer's history.

Why this one matters professionally

Strip away the noise and this is a process story, which is exactly why it is worth covering straight. Giving a creator a sneak peek at content is not unusual in itself — Behaviour's own first statement notes that. What made this one different, by Behaviour's own account, was speed without approval, a check that never happened, and a public reply that poured fuel on it instead of slowing it down.

The test now is not the statement. It is the next time a big streamer drops into the Fog: who approves the gift, what check happens first, and whether the public response matches the values the studio just restated.

GTA VI Pre-Load Moves Up to November 10

By Matt

Rockstar has moved GTA VI's digital pre-load up to Tuesday, November 10 — two days earlier than planned — and the download runs up to 212 GB depending on your device.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Rockstar has pulled GTA VI's digital pre-load forward. Instead of waiting until November 12, players can start downloading the game on Tuesday, November 10 — two extra days to get one of the biggest downloads in gaming history onto your console before launch.

What changed

When pre-orders opened, Rockstar gave November 12 as the pre-load date. That date has now moved up to Tuesday, November 10 — two days earlier than originally planned. The launch date itself is unchanged: GTA VI still arrives November 19, 2026.

The download: up to 212 GB

The pre-load needs up to 212 GB, depending on your device. That is a serious chunk of storage, so if your drive is close to full, now is the time to clear space — the earlier pre-load window gives you nine days between download and launch day to sort it out rather than scrambling at the last minute.

Senators at Sabres Over 6.5 (-118): Two Leaky Nets, Two Hot Attacks, One Obvious Total

By Matt

Ottawa's No. 1 goalie is on injured reserve, Buffalo's goalie sits at .874, both penalty kills rank in the bottom ten, and the total is 6.5. The over at -118 is the cleanest total on tonight's NHL board.

The game slip

Matchup: Ottawa Senators (9-6-1) at Buffalo Sabres (7-4-3)

Puck drop: Wednesday, November 4, 7:30 PM ET — ESPN+, KeyBank Center, Buffalo

Line: Sabres -125 (LetsBetMD) | Senators +110 (DraftKings/Fanatics) | Total 6.5, Over -118 (via FanDuel, per rg.org's morning prices)

Senators injury report: OUT — Linus Ullmark (lower body, injured reserve, targeting around November 7), Andre Burakovsky (lower body, injured reserve), Warren Foegele (knee, injured reserve), William Eklund (ankle, injured reserve). Ullmark is the one that changes this game: he has a .945 save percentage in eight starts, and he is not playing.

Sabres injury report: OUT — Matt Grzelcyk (lower body), Conor Timmins (lower body), Justin Danforth (upper body), Jack Rathbone (undisclosed), Konsta Helenius (upper body), Alex Lyon (upper body, days away from a return). DAY-TO-DAY — Jiri Kulich (concussion, expected back November 4). Zach Benson missed a practice last week on a maintenance day but returned to the ice Monday and is available for Wednesday's game. Buffalo's absences are mostly on defense, which is not what you want to hear when the total is 6.5.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. The goaltending gap is the whole bet. With Ullmark out, Ottawa turns to Samuel Ersson (.866) and Jonas Johansson (.848) — a long way from .945. Buffalo's Ukko-Pekka Luukkonen sits at .874 in nine starts. Neither net is stealing a low-scoring game tonight.

2. Neither penalty kill helps keep this under. Buffalo's kill ranks 28th at 68.6 percent; Ottawa's ranks 23rd at 72.9 percent. Power plays should produce, and both teams have the finishers to convert them — Tim Stutzle leads Ottawa with 21 points, and Tage Thompson leads Buffalo with 10 goals.

3. Buffalo has won three straight and is playing with confidence at home, while Ottawa is 9-6-1 and scores enough to keep up in a track meet. This is not a bet on either team winning — it is a bet that seven goals is a low bar for two offenses facing backup-level goaltending and bottom-ten penalty kills.

The bet

Over 6.5 goals (-118 at FanDuel). Seven goals is the ask, and the case is structural, not vibes: a .945 goalie is out, his replacements are at .866 and .848, the other goalie is at .874, and both penalty kills are in the bottom ten. The over is -118 at FanDuel, -120 at Fanatics and BetRivers, and -122 at DraftKings — shop it, but the number is the number.

The honest caveat: totals are the cruelest bets in hockey. One goalie gets hot for a night, one period goes scoreless on a handful of posts, and a 6-0 game can still stay under if the third period goes quiet. Seven goals is not a guarantee just because the goalies look shaky on paper — it is a probability play, and the probability is on the over's side tonight.

2-Leg Parlay (about +292): 76ers Moneyline + Senators-Sabres Over 6.5

By Matt

Two plays, no filler: the 7-1 76ers at plus money in Detroit, and seven goals in a game where both nets are leaky. Together at +112 and -118, they pay about +292.

The legs

Leg 1 — Philadelphia 76ers moneyline (+112 at FanDuel), at Detroit, 7:00 PM ET on ESPN. Philadelphia is 7-1, unbeaten on the road, and getting plus money against a 6-1 Detroit team missing starting center Jalen Duren (hamstring) and Isaiah Joe (hip). Joel Embiid is off the injury report after managing plantar fasciitis, and the 76ers' only loss this season came against Detroit in a game Embiid rested. This time he plays, Detroit's frontcourt has to handle him without Duren, and the better team is the underdog. Full game slip, injury reports for both teams, and the honest caveats are in the standalone 76ers post.

Leg 2 — Ottawa Senators at Buffalo Sabres, Over 6.5 goals (-118 at FanDuel), 7:30 PM ET on ESPN+. Ottawa's No. 1 goalie, Linus Ullmark (.945 in eight starts), is on injured reserve; his replacements sit at .866 and .848. Buffalo's Ukko-Pekka Luukkonen is at .874. Both penalty kills rank in the bottom ten (Buffalo 28th at 68.6 percent, Ottawa 23rd at 72.9 percent), and both teams have the scorers to punish that — Tim Stutzle leads Ottawa with 21 points, Tage Thompson leads Buffalo with 10 goals. Seven goals is a low bar for this matchup. Full game slip and injury reports are in the standalone Senators-Sabres post.

Why these two, together

A parlay only works if each leg clears on its own, and both of these do. The 76ers leg is a value moneyline on the better team at plus money. The total leg is a structural play on goaltending and penalty kills, not a guess about which team wins. Neither leg depends on the other, neither is a long shot dressed up as a lock, and there is no third leg added just to juice the payout.

At the morning prices (+112 and -118), the two legs combine to about +292. Books vary, so check your book's parlay price before placing it — the legs are the bet, not the exact combined number.

The honest caveat, stated plainly: parlays need everything to go right. Embiid's minutes could stay restricted and turn the Detroit game into a coin flip, and a hockey total can die on a single hot period from either goalie. If either leg makes you uncomfortable on its own, play it straight instead of parlaying it. That is always the right call.

New Era's Brewers World Series Collection Gives a First Title Three Distinct Looks

By Matt

New Era's Brewers World Series collection covers Milwaukee's first championship three ways: a black Locker Room set for the celebration, team-colored side-patch caps in six silhouettes, and a white Parade cap — already sold out in one style.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Milwaukee waited a long time for a first World Series title, and New Era is marking it the way only New Era can: with a lot of caps. The Brewers World Series collection, now listed at New Era's official store, gives the same milestone three distinct treatments — one for the celebration itself, one for everyday team colors, and one for the parade.

The three approaches

The Locker Room Collection is built around the championship's first moments. Its black 9FORTY A-Frame and 9TWENTY adjustable caps put "World Series Champions" across the front, with the Brewers logo centered in the composition — the look of the clubhouse celebration, made wearable.

The Side Patch designs take the opposite route: team-colored caps carrying commemorative side patches. Six silhouettes carry the treatment — the 59FIFTY, Low Profile 59FIFTY, 9FIFTY, 9SEVENTY Stretch Snap, 9FORTY M-Crown, and 9TWENTY — so the choice is about fit and format (fitted, snapback, or adjustable) without changing the central design idea.

Then there is the Parade cap, and it breaks away from both. The Parade 9FIFTY A-Frame uses a white crown and gray visor as a lighter setting for the Commissioner's Trophy, positioned behind an embroidered waving flag reading "World Champions '26." It is the most distinct piece in the collection — and, per the current listing, the one already marked sold out.

Why the hat story is the story here

Championship merch usually collapses into one design stamped on everything. What makes this collection worth noting is that it doesn't. The Locker Room set is for the night it happened. The side-patch set is for wearing a Brewers cap that happens to carry a World Series patch, long after the celebration. The Parade cap is for the day the city actually celebrates together. Three different jobs, three different caps, one title.

That matters more than usual for Milwaukee because this is a first. The Brewers beat the Tampa Bay Rays 4-1 in Game 5 to win the franchise's first World Series, and "2026" on a side patch is not a generic anniversary number — it is the year it finally happened. Availability varies by style, and the Parade cap's sold-out listing is a snapshot of retail status at a particular moment, not a claim about permanent rarity or future value. Check New Era's official store for current stock, pricing, and sizing before assuming any style is still there.

The bottom line for collectors

If you want the celebration look, the Locker Room set is the obvious pick. If you want a cap you will actually wear every week, the side-patch range — especially the 59FIFTY fitted — is the one built for that. And if you wanted the Parade cap, you may already be late: that white-and-gray A-Frame was the first to go. For a first championship, that is probably the most Milwaukee outcome of all — the parade cap selling out before the parade feeling has even faded.

The Elder Scrolls VI Has a Name at Last: Sentinel

By Matt

Eight years after The Elder Scrolls VI was first announced, Bethesda finally gave it a full title — The Elder Scrolls VI: Sentinel — in a video celebrating the studio's 40th anniversary. No release date, no gameplay, just a name — and a desert.

After more than eight years of silence, Bethesda finally said the word. The Elder Scrolls VI is called The Elder Scrolls VI: Sentinel, studio chief Todd Howard revealed on November 3 at the end of a video celebrating Bethesda Game Studios' 40th anniversary. No release date. No platforms. No gameplay. Just a title card, a few seconds of desert, and the loudest single word in gaming in years.

How the reveal actually happened

The announcement came in a video looking back at the studio's history, with Howard turning at the end toward Bethesda's future. The teaser that followed is brief: a camera looking down on the crest of a dune, the Bethesda Game Studios logo flashing as it zooms in, and the wind unearthing an Elder Scroll — complete with an inlaid blue gem and surrounding golden runes. The background cuts to black, the runes hang in the dark, and then the wordmark appears: The Elder Scrolls VI: Sentinel.

Xbox Wire posted the anniversary video and the name reveal on the morning of November 3. Bethesda's own follow-up was deliberately short: development continues, and it will be "some time" before there is more to share.

What the name tells us — and what it doesn't

Sentinel is the capital city of Hammerfell, the desert province on Tamriel's western coast, home of the Redguards. To Hammerfell's northeast is Skyrim; to its southeast is Cyrodiil, where Oblivion takes place. The dune in the teaser fits a province predominantly known for its deserts, and reports ahead of the reveal had already pointed to Hammerfell as the setting.

But be precise about what Bethesda actually confirmed: the title. The studio has not officially announced the game's setting, its story, or its platforms, and the teaser contains no combat, exploration, or gameplay footage of any kind. The Hammerfell reading is the overwhelming consensus of every outlet covering the reveal — and it is a very good reading — but it is still a reading, not a Bethesda announcement.

The teaser does drop one more lore breadcrumb. It closes with a quote attributed to Yazar, a Moth Priest in service to High Queen Iradne of Hammerfell, dated 4E 206: "The scroll offered only a shimmer, a veil thinly lifted to my eyes. A future lost. A victory undone. Sand of time scattered by fate." Skyrim begins in 4E 201, so a quote dated five years later strongly implies Sentinel takes place after Skyrim — not a prequel, and not set between earlier games. Whether the game itself is set in 4E 206 or later is still unclear, and Yazar and Iradne are best read as the first two names attached to the game, not confirmed characters with confirmed roles.

The wait is not over

Here is the cold water, and it matters. Bloomberg's Jason Schreier said on the day of the reveal that the game is still more than two years away. If that reporting holds, Sentinel does not launch before 2028 — a full decade after the game was first announced in June 2018. Bethesda has confirmed no window of its own, so treat 2028 as the earliest date consistent with Schreier's reporting, not as an official date.

That is the strange shape of this reveal: eight years to get a subtitle, and at least two more years to get the game. Fans spent the day celebrating a word, because after eight years, a word is progress. The 40th-anniversary video was a celebration of a studio's past — and, for the first time in years, a real signal about its future. It just isn't a release date, and nobody should pretend it is.

Why this one matters anyway

The Elder Scrolls is one of the few franchises where a title reveal alone moves the whole industry's calendar. Skyrim, released November 11, 2011, is approaching its 15th anniversary, and it has been re-released on essentially everything with a screen since. A new mainline Elder Scrolls game is not just another release — it is the kind of game other publishers schedule around. Sentinel's reveal, even as just a name, finally gives that future a shape: a desert province, a Redguard capital, a prophecy dated five years after Skyrim, and a wait that is measured in years, not months.

Sources: CBR: Sentinel setting and timeline placement revealed in new trailer · KeenGamer: Sentinel title and logo revealed in new trailer · Gadgets360: Bethesda confirms Sentinel title, launch reportedly more than 2 years away · Parade: Bethesda finally reveals Elder Scrolls 6 title after 8 years

Razer Unveils $350 Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition — Only 1,337 Units

By Matt

Razer's new Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition wraps a tournament-grade PS5 controller in a magnesium alloy exoskeleton — and only 1,337 serialized units will ever be made in this initial run.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Razer has unveiled the Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition, a limited-edition PlayStation 5 controller that turns its competitive flagship into a collector's piece. The price is $349.99, and the run is capped at 1,337 individually serialized units worldwide.

A magnesium exoskeleton

The headline is the shell. The Signature Edition swaps the standard body for a magnesium alloy exoskeleton with an open polygonal design across the top and back plates — you can see the internals through it. Chamfered edges and a nano coating keep debris out while leaving that open-air look intact.

The rest of the materials match the premium billing: a laser-etched Razer logo on a semi-transparent, tinted glass touchpad, matte black micro-textured TPU grips built to resist oils and abrasion, and double-shot PBT buttons so the legends never fade. The whole controller weighs just 260 grams.

Razer calls it its most ambitious PS5 controller to date, and the design celebrates a decade of Raiju development — the line debuted in 2016.

Same tournament-grade core

Underneath the new shell, it's the same competitive feature set as the standard Raiju V3 Pro:

• Contactless TMR thumbsticks in a symmetrical layout — anti-drift sensors for precise aim

• Six remappable multi-function buttons: two claw-grip bumpers and four mouse-click back buttons

• Up to 2,000Hz HyperPolling Wireless on PC for sharper input reporting

• Officially licensed for PS5, optimized for PC

The honest read, per the coverage: the $349.99 premium over the standard model (which launched at $220) buys materials and exclusivity, not new sensors or extra buttons. This is a statement piece that happens to be tournament-grade, not a performance upgrade over the base V3 Pro.

What's in the box

Every unit ships with a collector-grade kit: a leatherette carrying case, alternate thumbstick caps, four interchangeable back button covers, an anodized aluminum back button tool, and a HyperSpeed Wireless dongle.

When and where to get one

The Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition goes on sale November 10, exclusively at Razer and select RazerStores, in regionalized drops. Units are first-come, first-served, and Razer will share drop timing and access details on its social channels on November 6.

Two things worth knowing before you set an alarm: the 1,337 figure is described as an initial supply, so a future run isn't fully ruled out — and Razer hasn't said how units split by region, so buyers outside the US shouldn't assume local stock until allocation is confirmed. The timing is no accident either: the launch lands just before GTA 6, and Razer is clearly positioning this as the upgrade to have in hand for it.

2-Leg Parlay (~+111): Hurricanes ML + Lightning ML — The Two Best Home/Road Favorites on the Board

By Matt

Two favorites that should win outright: Carolina, the NHL's best team at 12-2-2 on a 10-game win streak, and Tampa Bay at home against a back-to-back Detroit team missing its captain. Together at -192 and -260, they pay about +111.

The legs

Leg 1 — Carolina Hurricanes moneyline (-192, FanDuel). The Hurricanes are 12-2-2, the defending champions, winners of 10 straight, with a goaltender in Pyotr Kochetkov who is 10-0-0 to open the season. Nashville is 5-4-5 and starting Juuse Saros at 3-3-4. Carolina doesn't need to cover anything here — just win, which is what this team does.

Leg 2 — Tampa Bay Lightning moneyline (-260, FanDuel). Tampa is 8-3-2 at home against a Detroit team on the second night of a back-to-back, coming off a physical win at Florida on Monday, and missing captain Dylan Larkin (injured reserve/suspension). Tampa's scorers — Kucherov, Hagel, Guentzel, Point — are all available, and numberFire gives the Lightning a 69.1 percent win probability.

The reasoning

Each leg clears on its own: Carolina is the best team in hockey against a .500 team, and Tampa is a -260 home favorite against a tired, short-handed visitor. That's the parlay discipline — no leg that needs a miracle, no leg that only works if the other one hits. At -192 and -260, the two together pay about +111 — a little better than even money for two favorites that should both win.

The honest caveat: parlays are still parlays. One bad night — Saros standing on his head, or Vasilevskiy's shaky .877 save percentage turning into another soft night — and the whole ticket is gone even if the other leg cashes easily. That's why this stays a two-legger: two favorites, no lottery tickets.

If gambling stops being fun, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

Collectibles · Nov 3, 2026 · 7:51 AM ET

FBI Warns Pokemon Card Shops About a New No-Payment Scam Hitting Stores

By Matt

The FBI's Newark office warned November 2 that scammers are targeting local card shops and Pokemon vendors with a scheme that lets them walk out with high-value cards without paying — contactless payments that never clear, and a phone app loaded with stolen credit cards.

The FBI is now, officially, a player in the Pokemon card hobby — and not in a fun way. The bureau's Newark, New Jersey, office warned on November 2 that a new scam is targeting local card shops and Pokemon TCG vendors, one designed to let bad actors buy high-value cards without spending any money at all.

How the scam works

According to the FBI notice, reported November 2, seven shops have been targeted so far. The scheme has two faces. First, thieves try to pay for cards via a contactless mobile payment that never actually clears — the transaction looks plausible at the counter and falls apart after the cards are already gone. Second, and more brazen, a cell phone app "operated by a criminal network that stores multiple stolen credit cards" is used to present payment that isn't theirs.

In one example cited in the warning, a man attempted to sell a box of trading cards to a store while an accomplice reached over the counter and stole additional cards — the old distraction play, dressed up for a hobby where a single card can be worth thousands.

Why now, and why Pokemon

The warning lands weeks after the Department of Justice announced an investigation into the biggest Pokemon card heist ever recorded, and it fits a pattern the hobby has felt all year: as card values have climbed, robberies and fraud targeting shops have climbed with them. A shop's singles wall is, functionally, a jewelry case that the public is invited to browse.

For collectors, the practical takeaway is boring and useful: buy from shops you know, be suspicious of deals that route around normal payment, and if you're selling, don't let a "payment" walk out the door with the card before it clears. For shops, the FBI's message is the same one the hobby keeps learning the expensive way — the cards are worth real money now, and the people targeting them know it.

This is also the week the hobby's calendar turns: Pokemon's November slate — the Delta Reign expansion and the 30th Celebration Day & Night Ultra-Premium Collections — lands November 6, which means foot traffic and high-value inventory in shops are both about to spike. That's exactly the moment a warning like this matters most.

Sources: FBI Issues Surprise Official 'Pokemon' Trading Card Warning, Every Pokemon TCG Product Releasing in November 2026

November's PlayStation Plus Monthly Games are claimable today through November 30: Dead Island 2, Tomb Raider IV-VI Remastered, and Monster Train 2. Because the Tomb Raider collection holds three games, the month technically delivers five games for the subscription you're already paying for.

November's PlayStation Plus Monthly Games are live today, and this is one of the stronger months of the year. Sony announced the lineup on October 28 on the PlayStation Blog, and as of today, November 3, all three titles are claimable through Monday, November 30, for members at every tier — Essential, Extra and Premium alike. Claim them in that window and they stay playable for as long as your subscription stays active.

The lineup

Dead Island 2 (PS5, PS4) is the headline. Dambuster Studios' first-person zombie action RPG drops you in HELL-A — a quarantined, blood-soaked Los Angeles — for melee combat built around the FLESH system, which renders zombie damage in spectacularly gross detail, solo or in three-player online co-op.

Tomb Raider IV-VI Remastered (PS5, PS4) is the three-for-one. Aspyr's collection bundles the whole Darkness Trilogy: The Last Revelation (Lara in Egypt, racing her mentor Von Croy to an ancient tomb), Chronicles (a globe-trotting set of flashback adventures told after Lara is presumed dead), and The Angel of Darkness (the darker, divisive Paris-and-Prague chapter) — with a toggle between original and remastered visuals.

Monster Train 2 (PS5 only) is the one gap for PS4-only subscribers: there is no PS4 version in this offer. The deckbuilding roguelite sequel has you leading clans of former angels and demons aboard trains through Hell, Heaven and the Abyss, with five new clans in the mix.

The fine print, and the regional wrinkle

October's games — F1 25, Hunt: Showdown 1896 and Earth Defense Force: World Brothers 2 — left the claim list after Monday, November 2, so if you missed that deadline, they're gone. November's window runs the other way: claim by November 30, play whenever.

One regional note: subscribers in Japan get Secret Neighbor in place of Dead Island 2, a Japan-only swap. Everyone else gets the lineup as listed. Sony has not yet detailed November's Extra and Premium Game Catalog additions — those usually land mid-month — but the Monthly Games trio alone makes this a five-game month in practice, and Dead Island 2 is the kind of big-budget open-world game that usually anchors a month on its own.

The game

Gravhounds is built around a pack, not a lone hero. One to four players take control of bio-engineered canine workers and head out onto hostile planets — the premise is right there in the pitch: good dogs, hostile planets.

One Piece Sets Sail in Fortnite: Treasure Hunters Is Live

By Matt

The Straw Hats are in Fortnite. Treasure Hunters launched November 1 with a new 80-player island, Devil Fruit Mythics, and the One Piece crew rolling out in waves through mid-November.

The wait is over, and the Going Merry has replaced the Battle Bus. Fortnite's pirate mini-season, Treasure Hunters, launched Sunday, November 1 with update v43.00, and its centerpiece is the One Piece crossover fans have wanted for years — the first time the anime has gotten a dedicated Fortnite season.

This is not a skins-only collab. One Piece is built into how the season plays.

A new island built for a treasure hunt

Chapter 7 Season 4, Override, is out. In its place is Crossbones Isle, a skull-shaped island that holds 80 players instead of the usual 100, and the whole season is structured around the chase: track targets, crack chests, beat rival crews, and claim treasure before anyone else does. Sprites are staying too, with new variants and Mastery Mondays.

Two of the island's locations come straight out of the anime: Arlong Park and Windmill Village. And the season's opening shot says everything about the tone — the Going Merry sailing through fog under a full moon, carrying players into the drop.

The powers are the point

The gameplay trailer, which dropped October 30, shows the One Piece abilities working as Mythic items rather than cosmetic nods. Luffy's Gum-Gum Fruit gives you the stretchy rubber attacks, including the Gum-Gum Pistol. Ace's Flame-Flame Fruit brings the fire. Zoro's Three Sword Style and Usopp's slingshot are in as Mythics too, alongside a tangerine healing item for the Nami fans.

That is what separates this from most crossovers. You are not just dressed as the Straw Hats. You are fighting like them.

Who you can play, and when

Here is the part that tripped a lot of people up: only two One Piece characters are in the Battle Pass. Portgas D. Ace and Nico Robin headline the pass (reported at 800 V-Bucks), alongside a new original character called Captain X — the voice you hear at the One Piece chest on the island — and anime-style remixes of Jonesy, Cuddle Team Leader, and Charlotte.

The rest of the crew comes through the Item Shop in waves:

• November 1 (launch day): Monkey D. Luffy, Nami, and Usopp

• November 7: Roronoa Zoro, Sanji, and the Chopper Sidekick

• November 13: Buggy

So if you want the full crew, pace your V-Bucks. Luffy is not in the pass, and Zoro and Sanji are a week out.

The season keeps rolling

Treasure Hunters is a mini-season — about five weeks, closing with a Chapter 7 live event on December 5 — and it does not stand still. Each week brings a new captain with their own crew and point of interest, on what Epic calls Captain's Days: November 1, 7, 12, 14, and 21, each expanding the loot pool with new weapons and Sprites. The Straw Hats and A$AP Rocky — who has his own battleship on the map and a signature Shock Rocket — are first. Lil Uzi Vert follows, and Cap'n Hatsune Miku and Kasane Teto close out the lineup.

One Piece is not stopping at Fortnite, either. Rocket League's limited-time event runs November 1 through November 23, with challenges unlocking a Luffy Straw Hat Topper, a Straw Hat Crew Boost, and a Going Merry Sea Trail.

Watch the trailer

The official gameplay trailer is the best way to see the season's loop in action — the treasure hunt, the crews, and the Devil Fruit powers all in one place:

Watch on YouTube

If the embed does not load, watch it directly on YouTube: Fortnite Treasure Hunters gameplay trailer.

The season is live right now, and Luffy, Nami, and Usopp are already in the shop. The only question is whether you are dropping Arlong Park first — or going straight for the treasure.

By Matt

Embark Studios put ARC Raiders' first year at six billion matches played, with the extraction shooter passing 16.3 million global sales in its first nine months.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

One year in, ARC Raiders has put up numbers most games never reach. Embark Studios put the extraction shooter's first year at six billion matches played — an average of 16 million raids a day — and the game passed 16.3 million global sales in its first nine months.

The numbers

Matches played in year one: Six billion

Average raids per day: 16 million

Global sales in first nine months: 16.3 million

What the year means

Six billion rounds is not a launch spike — it is a sustained habit. ARC Raiders has held a daily audience large enough to average 16 million raids every single day for a year, and the sales figure shows that audience kept growing well past launch: 16.3 million copies in nine months is a commercial result most shooters never touch.

The first-year totals also frame what comes next. A game with this kind of daily volume does not need to win players over anymore — it needs to keep giving them reasons to come back, and Embark now has a year of proof that the loop works.

Nagoshi Studio Is Officially Dead — and So Is Gang of Dragon

By Matt

Toshihiro Nagoshi's NetEase-backed studio was dissolved by shareholder vote on October 31, ending Gang of Dragon, the Yakuza-style action game announced at The Game Awards last December.

It is official now, in the least ceremonious way possible: Nagoshi Studio is gone, and the game it was built to make is gone with it. A notice in Kanpo, the Japanese government gazette, confirms the studio was dissolved by a shareholders' resolution on October 31, 2026 — ending development of Gang of Dragon, the melee-based action-adventure set in Shinjuku, Tokyo, that was announced at The Game Awards in December 2025.

The news had been expected for months, but the quiet of it is striking. There was no farewell statement, no social post, no final trailer. The studio simply faded: its YouTube channel and official website disappeared over the summer, co-head Daisuke Sato changed his bio to "Ex-Nagoshi Studios," and its Google Business listing was marked permanently closed.

How it fell apart

Nagoshi Studio was founded by Toshihiro Nagoshi, the creator behind Yakuza and Like a Dragon, under NetEase's umbrella — an attempt to build a new contender and evolution for that series. Gang of Dragon starred Ma Dong-seok (Don Lee) and was pitched as a hard-hitting, street-level action game in the tradition Nagoshi built his name on.

The money ran out first. Reports dating back to March 2026 said NetEase pulled funding after it emerged that another ¥7 billion — roughly $44 million — would be needed to finish the game and bring it to market. For all of NetEase's size, that was apparently the line.

Why it stings

This is not just another cancelled game. Nagoshi is one of the most important creators in Japanese games, and Gang of Dragon was supposed to be his second act — a new studio, a new publisher backer, a new world, with one of Korea's biggest action stars attached. Instead, it becomes a cautionary tale about how quickly even a legendary name can lose its runway when the funding math stops working.

The game was only revealed a few months before the money stopped. Fans never got a release date, never got a playable demo, never really got more than the promise. Now they will not get the game at all.

Tom Brady's Card Vault Opens Its 20th Store — First in Pennsylvania, in Pittsburgh's Strip District

By Matt

Card Vault by Tom Brady opened its first Pennsylvania location Sunday at The Terminal in Pittsburgh's Strip District — its 20th store nationwide, with fans lining up before the doors opened.

The hobby's retail boom has a new address. Card Vault by Tom Brady opened its first Pennsylvania store on Sunday at The Terminal in Pittsburgh's Strip District, its 20th location nationwide, with fans lining up before the opening to get a first look.

The timing was deliberate: the grand opening was scheduled to coincide with a major Pittsburgh sports weekend, and the Strip District — already one of the city's busiest foot-traffic corridors — gave the store a ready-made crowd.

What the store offers

The Pittsburgh location carries singles, sealed boxes, packs and collectibles from Topps, Panini, Upper Deck, Pokémon and Fanatics Authentic, according to the store announcement. Customers can also submit cards for professional grading through PSA, Beckett and SGC — the three names that matter most to anyone trying to turn a pull into a graded asset.

The location also features Card Vault's signature vault: a rotating selection of autographed and authenticated memorabilia, including helmets, bats, balls and other pieces. That vault concept is the brand's calling card — part retail floor, part museum case — and it is a big reason the chain has scaled to 20 stores.

Why this opening matters

Brady did not attend the grand opening, though he is expected to visit the Pittsburgh location in the near future. His name on the door is the point, though: Card Vault is the most celebrity-backed retail bet in the hobby, and its expansion into Pennsylvania — a state with deep sports-card roots and a Pittsburgh collector scene that has been waiting for a flagship — is a signal of how mainstream the modern card shop has become.

Twenty stores is not a pop-up experiment anymore. It is a chain, and Pittsburgh is the latest proof that the card boom did not end when the pandemic-era frenzy cooled. It just got a storefront.

Gaming · Nov 2, 2026 · 7:50 AM ET

Dead by Daylight Apologizes After TheBurntPeanut Stream Sparks a Weekend Backlash

By Matt

Behaviour Interactive apologized November 1 after a staff member gifted streamer TheBurntPeanut free access to Dead by Daylight's full roster during a Halloween stream — a decision the studio says was made without proper approval.

Halloween weekend brought Dead by Daylight an unwelcome kind of attention. Behaviour Interactive apologized on November 1 after a staff member gifted popular Twitch streamer TheBurntPeanut free access to the game's entire roster of Survivors and Killers during a Halloween stream — setting off a community backlash that ended with the studio admitting it handled the whole thing badly.

The sequence, as reported by Dexerto and Games.gg, went like this. TheBurntPeanut, who built his audience largely on extraction shooters like Arc Raiders, played Dead by Daylight for the first time on stream and discovered he had been given instant access to dozens of characters that regular players unlock over time with in-game currency. "Guys! I just got all the guys!" he said on stream, joking about "streamer privileges."

Then the game's official Twitch account weighed in with a blunt message: "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT! THEY ARE YOURS TO KEEP!"

The apology — and why it did not land cleanly

The backlash had two layers: objections to Behaviour supporting TheBurntPeanut, including criticism of his past on-stream language, and anger at the dismissive response from the official account. Some players posted that they had uninstalled the game as the dispute escalated.

Behaviour's November 1 statement, posted on DbD Pulse, its community account, identified two failures: the decision to award the content was made without the appropriate approvals, and when concerns were raised during the stream, the team should have investigated them instead of dismissing them. The studio also condemned inflammatory language directed at members of the community and said threats against employees were unacceptable, adding that it has begun reviewing its internal processes to prevent a repeat.

Even the apology drew criticism — for appearing on the smaller DbD Pulse account rather than the main Dead by Daylight account, which has 1.3 million followers. For a community that has stuck with the game through ten years of service, the placement read as part of the problem: the loud response went on the big stage, the apology on the small one.

What to watch

Giving creators a sneak peek at content is not unusual in itself — Behaviour's own statement notes that — and this story is really about process: who approved it, how concerns were handled in the moment, and where the accountability shows up afterward. The studio says its review is already underway. The community, judging by the weekend, will be watching whether anything actually changes the next time a big streamer drops into the Fog.

• Dexerto — Dead by Daylight team respond following backlash to TheBurntPeanut receiving free DLC

• Games.gg — TheBurntPeanut's Dead by Daylight stream sparks studio apology

• GameBastion — Dead by Daylight devs apologize after TheBurntPeanut stirs controversy

Pokémon Returns to Hoenn on November 6 With Mega Evolution: Delta Reign

By Matt

Pokémon TCG: Mega Evolution — Delta Reign launches November 6 with 135+ cards, headlined by Mega Rayquaza ex's first appearance since 2015, plus a new Legendary Stadium mechanic and retailer-exclusive Hoenn promos.

Pokémon is going back to Hoenn. On Friday, November 6, the Pokémon TCG launches Mega Evolution — Delta Reign, the set that brings Generation 3's most iconic Legendaries back to the card table as part of the franchise's 30th anniversary celebrations — and it is shaping up to be the biggest TCG launch of the month.

The set

Delta Reign packs over 135 cards, led by Mega Rayquaza ex — appearing in the TCG for the first time since 2015's XY—Ancient Origins expansion. Rayquaza is joined by Mega Golurk ex, Mega Malamar ex and Mega Golisopod ex, plus 14 illustration rares and six special illustration rare Pokémon and Supporter cards.

The set's headline mechanic is Legendary Stadium: three Legendary Stadiums that can be combined into a single connected illustration, adding a new strategic wrinkle for players and a display-piece chase for collectors. Booster packs, Elite Trainer Boxes and other collections arrive through participating retailers, and the set hits Pokémon TCG Live a day early, on November 5.

The Hoenn promos

The Hoenn homecoming extends beyond the packs. Starting November 6, fans who spend $15 or more on Pokémon TCG products at participating retailers receive a promotional card, and the Legendary you get depends on where you shop:

• Kyogre — Hot Topic, Barnes & Noble, Five Below and Macy's

• Groudon — GameStop

• Giratina — Best Buy

All three promos carry the exclusive Pokémon 30 treatment, tying them to the anniversary year. For completionists, that means at least three stops — and for everyone else, a good excuse to pick a favorite Legendary and plan accordingly.

The same-day competition

Delta Reign doesn't arrive alone. November 6 also brings the Pokémon 30th Celebration Ultra-Premium Collections — one built around Espeon ex, one around Umbreon ex — each packing 29 30th Celebration packs, a Classic Collection pack, a Pikachu ex promo, the Espeon or Umbreon ex promo, a playmat and a metal coin. Same day, same anniversary, same crowded shelf: it is the biggest single Friday the hobby has seen in a while, and it lands right as the McDonald's Pokémon Happy Meal promotion (November 10) is about to send the whole franchise into overdrive.

If you're planning to hunt Delta Reign on release day, get there early. The Hoenn nostalgia is real, the Rayquaza chase is real, and November's card calendar only gets busier from here.

Nintendo Declares November 1 Pikmin Day in a Surprise Direct

By Matt

Nintendo's surprise nine-minute Pikmin Day Direct on October 31 officially designated November 1 as Pikmin Day, with a free Pikmin 4 Switch 2 demo landing November 2 ahead of the full game's November 12 launch.

Nintendo loves a surprise, and this one arrived with almost no warning. On October 31, a roughly nine-minute Pikmin Day Direct quietly appeared on Nintendo's YouTube channels — not streamed live, announced only shortly beforehand — and it came with a piece of official franchise history: November 1 is now Pikmin Day.

Pikmin creator Shigeru Miyamoto opened the presentation with the reasoning, and it is delightfully on-brand: the original Red, Blue and Yellow Pikmin standing side by side resemble the three 1s in "11/1." Starting this year, November 1 belongs to the little guys.

Everything the Direct announced

No new Pikmin game was announced — Nintendo was clear about that, and the Direct's real job was setting the table for the Switch 2 edition of Pikmin 4. Here's the full rundown:

• Free Pikmin 4 Switch 2 demo — launching November 2, covering the opening Sunspeckled Terrace area and two Dandori Academy stages. Save data carries over to the full game.

• Pikmin 4: Nintendo Switch 2 Edition + Dandori Academy — launches November 12 for $69.99, with a $9.99 Upgrade Pack for owners of the Switch 1 version. The edition adds improved visuals and voice commands for Oatchi.

• Hello! Pikmin short film series — a new animated series from Nintendo Pictures, with the first short debuting in the Direct, a second arriving November 1, and new shorts monthly from December through the Nintendo Today! app.

• Pikmin Bloom Secret Caves — coming winter 2026, letting players obtain cave maps and send Pikmin underground in search of treasure. Finishing Dandori Academy exams in the Switch 2 edition unlocks special cave maps.

• Nintendo Treehouse Plays: Pikmin 4 — streaming November 1 at 9 a.m. PT.

Why a Pikmin Day matters

Nintendo has devoted serious airtime to Pikmin this year — the Switch 2 edition was announced in the September Direct — and a dedicated day is the company planting a flag for one of its most beloved, least blockbuster franchises. Pikmin has never been a Mario-sized seller, but it has one of the most devoted fanbases in gaming, and a yearly date on the calendar is how cult favorites become traditions.

The demo-first strategy is smart, too. A free taste on November 2, ten days before launch, with progress carrying over, is the lowest-friction way to turn curious Switch 2 owners into day-one buyers. If you have ever wondered what the Pikmin fuss is about, Sunday's Treehouse stream and Monday's demo are the on-ramp.

Happy Pikmin Day. The Red, Blue and Yellow ones are waiting.

The $50 Million Mickey Mantle Sale That Wasn't — Goldin Calls the Claim 'False News'

By Matt

A podcast claim that a PSA 10 1952 Topps Mickey Mantle sold privately for $50 million sent the hobby into a frenzy — until Goldin founder Ken Goldin shot it down 'with 100% fact' and Darren Rovell reported the deal was contemplated but never consummated.

The hobby got its biggest headline of the year this week — and then the headline got debunked within hours.

Entrepreneur and collector Patrick Bet-David said on his podcast that one of the three PSA 10 examples of the iconic 1952 Topps Mickey Mantle #311 had traded privately for $50 million. Had it been real, it would have been the most expensive trading card ever sold by an enormous margin. The claim spread fast. Then the pushback arrived even faster.

"That is false news"

Ken Goldin, founder of Goldin auctions, responded directly on X: "That is false news. An aggressive rumor that is not true; I can state that with 100% fact. A 1952 mantle has NOT sold for $50 million."

Sports business reporter Darren Rovell added the nuance that explains how the rumor likely started: "While this transaction has been more than contemplated, I have been told that it has not been consummated." In other words, a deal involving one of the three PSA 10 Mantles may have been discussed or negotiated — but no money changed hands, no ownership transferred, no buyer or seller has been publicly identified, and no auction house has confirmed any sale.

Why $50 million never passed the smell test

The card's rarity is real: PSA's population report lists only three copies of the 1952 Topps Mantle graded Gem Mint 10, with none graded higher. But the math never worked. The highest recorded public sale for a 1952 Mantle is the CGC-graded 9.5 version that sold for $12.6 million through Heritage Auctions on August 28, 2022. A jump from $12.6 million to $50 million in four years — with no public confirmation from any party to the deal — was always going to need more than a podcast mention.

The episode is a useful reminder for a hobby running hot: the difference between a contemplated deal and a completed one is everything, and the biggest number in the room is not always a sale. The record for a Mantle remains $12.6 million. The $50 million card, for now, exists only as a rumor.

Sources: Men's Journal, Collectibles News

Pokémon's Dual Fusion Is Live — and the Espeon and Umbreon Decks Land Tomorrow

By Matt

Pokémon TCG Pocket's Dual Fusion expansion is out today with the game's first dual-type cards — Mega Mewtwo X ex and Mega Greninja ex lead the set — and tomorrow the physical game gets its 30th Celebration battle decks, Espeon ex and Umbreon ex, ready to play out of the box for $39.99.

Pokémon's 30th anniversary year keeps stacking releases, and this week is one of the biggest yet — on both sides of the game. The app gets its first dual-type cards today. The physical game gets two ready-to-play decks tomorrow. Here's the full picture.

Dual Fusion is live today

Pokémon TCG Pocket's Dual Fusion expansion launched today, and its headline mechanic is something the app has never had before: dual-type cards. One card, two types at once. The two stars are Mega Mewtwo X ex (Psychic and Fighting) and Mega Greninja ex (Water and Darkness), split across two booster packs — one for Mega Mewtwo X, one for Pikachu — with new versions of Pikachu ex, Mew ex, Venusaur, Charizard, Blastoise, and Mega Dragonite ex in the mix.

For players, dual-type cards change deckbuilding in a real way. A Psychic/Fighting attacker doesn't just hit harder — it opens lines your deck couldn't run before, because a single card can answer matchups that used to need two different attackers. That's the kind of mechanic that reshuffles a meta overnight, and it's why this set is getting more attention than a normal Pocket drop.

The launch comes with a lot around it. Six Dual Fusion rental decks are free to play through December 15, so anyone can try the new cards without building from scratch. A Special Pack called First Partners runs October 31 to December 26, featuring the first partner Pokémon from all nine regions. And second anniversary events run from today into mid-December, with an anniversary ticket exchangeable for a booster pack with a guaranteed high-rarity card.

The bigger structural news is the schedule change that arrives with the new C Series: sets move from roughly a four-week cycle to roughly six weeks. The developers said it plainly — players told them big sets were making collecting hard. Collectors asked for breathing room, and they got it. That's a rare thing in a live game: a publisher slowing down because the players asked.

The Espeon and Umbreon decks land tomorrow

If physical cards are more your thing, tomorrow (October 30) brings the Pokémon TCG: 30th Celebration Battle Decks — Espeon ex and Umbreon ex. Each is a ready-to-play, all-foil 60-card deck for $39.99, with a playmat, coin, and deck box in the box. The Espeon ex deck runs with Victini; the Umbreon ex deck runs with Zeraora. No chasing packs to play — open it and you're in a game.

That's the part worth noticing. In a year full of chase products and 30 different Pikachu variants to collect, a pair of decks built for actually playing — every card foil, everything you need in the box — is a different kind of release. It's aimed at the person who wants to sit down and play tonight, not the person hunting a pull. For a 30th anniversary celebration, that's a good look: the game remembering it's a game.

Sources: Shane the Gamer, Gaming Route, GameTrader.SG, CBR, CBR, Pokémon.com

Pokémon Is Back at McDonald's — and the Promo Accidentally Spoiled Next Year's Cards Too

By Matt

McDonald's confirmed its Pokémon Happy Meal returns in the US on November 10 with 15 toys and 15 trading cards — and, in announcing it, reportedly showed off next year's M27 set a full year early.

The Pokémon Happy Meal is back — and this time the announcement itself became a collectible story.

McDonald's has confirmed the return of its Pokémon Happy Meal, launching in the US on November 10 with a 15-piece toy set and a 15-card trading card collection, plus a digital game built into the packaging. The international rollout follows through February 2027, starting with Canada, Australia and several other markets in November.

What's in the box

The toys are miniature Happy Meal boxes, each carrying the face of a different Pokémon — and they do more than sit on a shelf. McDonald's executive Guillaume Huin showed that each box-shaped toy converts into a bag charm you can clip onto keys, bags, or just about anything.

Confirmed headliners so far: Pikachu, Eevee, Charmander, Squirtle and Umbreon, with promotional artwork also showing Piplup. The card side includes holofoil cards, with Jirachi, Pikachu and Absol among the previews — though McDonald's has not detailed the complete 15-card checklist yet. Fifteen cards to collect, fifteen toys to chase: the classic Happy Meal math, with a charm twist collectors didn't see coming.

The accidental spoiler

Here's the part collectors are actually talking about: when Pokémon officially confirmed this year's cards, the post reportedly showed an entirely different set — stamped M27, not M26 — before it was quickly taken down.

That M27 set, per reporting on the since-removed post, includes Raichu and Rowlet among its 15 cards, with a new Squirtle card as one of the notable fresh designs. If those really are next year's Happy Meal cards, they were revealed roughly a year early — and they carry an interesting implication: with no sign of new Pokémon in the set, the next Pokémon games (Winds & Waves) may not arrive until later in 2027, since McDonald's sets historically include the newest starters.

Treat the M27 details as reported, not officially confirmed — the post came down fast, and McDonald's hasn't confirmed next year's set. But the M26 set itself is locked: November 10 in the US, 15 toys, 15 cards, and a charm for your bag.

Why collectors care

McDonald's Pokémon promos are one of the hobby's great equalizers — cheap, everywhere, and genuinely fun to chase. A 15-and-15 set with a usable charm gimmick is already a strong one. An accidental year-early leak of the next set on top of it? That's the kind of story the hobby talks about for months.

Sources: Hypebeast, TheGamer

Collectibles · Oct 29, 2026 · 9:38 PM ET

Ohtani's $3.24M Logoman Leads a Monster Week for Sports Cards

By Matt

Shohei Ohtani's 1/1 Logoman patch auto sold for $3.24 million — the most expensive Dodgers card ever — headlining a week that also saw a $2.16M Cooper Flagg, a record Messi Topps NOW, and a $1.47M vintage football set.

It's been a monster week for sports cards, the kind where the top of the market moves in a bunch of directions at once: modern superstars, a farewell record, and vintage all putting up numbers in the same seven days.

Ohtani's $3.24 million Logoman

The headline is Shohei Ohtani's 2025 Topps MLB MVP The Game Dynasty Black Auto Patch 1/1, graded PSA 9, which sold for $3.24 million through Fanatics Premier on October 23. It's the most expensive Los Angeles Dodgers card ever sold at auction, and the third-biggest Ohtani sale of all time.

Logoman cards are the hobby's ultimate chase for a reason: the patch is cut from a game-worn jersey's actual MLB logo, so there's exactly one of each. A 1/1 Logoman auto of the game's biggest star, in a set built to honor one of the greatest performances in North American sports history, is the kind of card that doesn't come around twice. The $3.24 million price says the market agrees — even in a week where Ohtani's on-field postseason was quiet, the cardboard didn't care.

Flagg and the dual Logoman right behind it

The same Fanatics Premier run put two more seven-figure cards on the board. A 2025 Topps Motif Logoman Auto 1/1 Cooper Flagg (unscratched redemption) sold for $2.16 million — the week's second-biggest sale and a statement about where the hobby thinks Flagg's market is going. And a 2026 Topps Chrome Dual MVP Gold Logoman Auto 1/1 of Aaron Judge and Shohei Ohtani went for $1.23 million. Two of the game's biggest names on one card, one copy in the world, seven figures. That's the modern high-end market in one line.

Messi's farewell sets a different kind of record

The record that isn't an auction at all might be the most telling one. Lionel Messi's Topps NOW "Final Farewell" card is now the best-selling Topps NOW card of all time at 664,325 copies sold, passing Shohei Ohtani's 50/50 card (653,737). No bidding, no 1/1 — just an enormous number of people buying the same farewell card in the same week.

That's the other side of the modern hobby: retirements and historic moments have become instant mass-market events. One buyer also gets a 1-of-1 autographed White OpalFractor version, and the card has Chrome and Opal Chrome parallels including a 1-of-1 SuperFractor — so the chase exists inside the mass release. Fanatics, which now controls the exclusive trading card rights for MLB, NBA, NFL, and FIFA World Cup properties, has turned the farewell card into its own category.

Vintage: the $1.47 million football set

And vintage had its moment too. A nearly complete 1962 Topps football set — 175 of the 176 cards, the top set on PSA's All-Time Finest registry at a 9.337 grade point average — sold for $1,473,638 in Mile High Card Company's Fall Auction, the highest price ever paid for a football card set. Only about 1% of all submissions from the set have ever earned a PSA 9, and 101 of those are in this collection. The auction also moved a $360,913 baseball collection and an $833,138 Babe Ruth card (a 1933 Sport Kings, SGC 9, the auction's highest-selling individual card).

Put it together and the week tells one story: the hobby's top end is not cooling off. Modern 1/1s are doing millions, farewell cards are doing hundreds of thousands of copies, and the best vintage sets are setting records of their own. Different corners, same heat.

Sources: Sports Illustrated, Men's Journal, Yardbarker, Sports Collectors Daily

Salomon and Protect Drop an ACS NEXT Collab

By Matt

The ACS NEXT PROTECT: SLUSHIE goes on sale today, October 28, exclusively through Protect's website, in the 'Lava Falls/Cyber Yellow' colorway.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The shoe

Salomon has teamed up with UK streetwear brand Protect on a new colorway of the ACS NEXT: the ACS NEXT PROTECT: SLUSHIE. It goes on sale today, October 28, exclusively through Protect's website. The collaboration was first detailed on October 23.

The colorway

The colorway is called "Lava Falls/Cyber Yellow." It pairs slushie-inspired gradients with bright yellow details — a bold, nostalgia-driven look on one of Salomon's most progressive silhouettes.

Where to buy

Shoe: ACS NEXT PROTECT: SLUSHIE

Where: Exclusively through Protect's website

Kylie Jenner's Khy After Dark Winter Collection Launches Today

By Matt

Kylie Jenner's Khy drops its 16-piece After Dark winter collection today — statement outerwear, sculptural silhouettes, faux fur and animal prints, following her Victoria's Secret runway debut earlier this month.

The drop

Kylie Jenner's clothing label Khy releases its winter collection, Khy After Dark, today, October 27. The 16-piece lineup leans dark: statement outerwear, sculptural silhouettes, faux-fur details, oversized bomber jackets, knitwear and animal prints.

The campaign

Jenner stars in the campaign herself, photographed by Quentin de Briey in the hills of Los Angeles. Outerwear is the center of the collection, per ELLE's preview — a darker aesthetic for the brand's latest winter release.

The timing

The drop follows Jenner's runway debut at the Victoria's Secret Fashion Show earlier this month — Khy's founder stepping onto the runway herself, then immediately putting a winter collection behind it.

Why it matters

• Khy After Dark is the brand's winter statement piece — outerwear-led, texture-heavy, and timed to the cold-weather buy.

• The Victoria's Secret debut-to-drop pipeline shows how fast Jenner is moving the brand from label to runway presence.

• Sixteen pieces keeps it tight: a focused winter edit, not a sprawling catalog.

Sources: Kylie Jenner Reveals Khy After Dark Collection Following Victoria's Secret Debut (Fashion Times, citing ELLE's preview).

Four of Six October Drops Got About a Third Cheaper

By Matt

Waiting paid off on most of October's big sneaker drops — four of six pairs got about a third cheaper on resale, while two held firm.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The pattern

For most of October's big releases, waiting paid. Four of six October drops got about a third cheaper on the resale market, according to the price tracking.

The mover

The Air Jordan 12 "Eggnog" fell from a $299 first resale ask to $179 by October 24 — a drop of roughly 40%, and a clear case of the pre-release price collapsing before release day.

Air Jordan 12 "Eggnog": $299; $179; Down about 40%

The holder

Not everything fell. The Pharrell x adidas Adistar Jellyfish "Red" did not move at all — the exception that proves the rule, and a reminder that heat still holds its price on the right pair.

The takeaway

The split is the story: four of six down about a third, two flat. If you were deciding whether to buy before release or wait, October's numbers say patience was usually — but not always — rewarded.
